The dentate runs the expansion-recoding sparsifier motif; CA3 runs the recurrent autoassociator motif; CA1 runs the comparator motif; hippocampus and cortex jointly run the two-store complementary-learning motif; and the whole loop rides the theta-gamma nested-oscillator motif of the microcircuits catalogue.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Encoding is organised by the 4 to 8 hertz theta rhythm carrying 30 to 100 hertz gamma packets, with one-shot synaptic change inside a single theta cycle.

Consolidation runs offline in sharp-wave ripples, 80 to 250 millisecond events at 100 to 250 hertz, replaying waking sequences at roughly 10 to 20 times real speed.

Systems consolidation to cortex takes weeks in rodents and years in humans.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

The human hippocampus holds on the order of 40 million neurons total across both sides.

Behavioural estimates suggest thousands of distinct episodes encodable per day but steep normal forgetting, with roughly half of unrehearsed detail lost within days, following the Ebbinghaus curve.

Retrieval latency for a cued autobiographical event is on the order of 1 to 10 seconds.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Acetylcholine from the medial septum sets the encode-versus-retrieve balance, high during attentive waking to favour storage.

Dopamine and noradrenaline mark novelty and arousal, gating which events earn lasting synaptic change.

The anterior thalamus acts as the loop's gate: without its permissive relay the hippocampal index cannot drive cortical recall.

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

This is the system in which long-term potentiation was discovered, at the perforant path in 1973, and the hippocampus remains the tissue with the fastest and largest synaptic change in the brain, plus adult neurogenesis in the dentate gyrus at a debated but non-zero human rate.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The circuit is anatomically complete at birth but functionally late: infantile amnesia reflects immature dentate and prefrontal stations, and reliable autobiographical memory appears around ages three to four.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

The hippocampus is ancient medial pallium, present in all vertebrates as a spatial mapper; the episodic extension - place plus time plus self - is an elaboration of that navigation machinery, which is why human memory palaces work.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

The hippocampus carries the brain's densest cortisol receptor load and inhibits the stress axis; chronic cortisol excess shrinks it, as in Cushing disease, while oestrogen and thyroid hormone modulate its plasticity, and thiamine deficiency destroys its diencephalic partners.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Awake, the system encodes under cholinergic drive; in slow-wave sleep it replays via sharp-wave ripples nested in cortical slow oscillations and spindles, performing the nightly copy to cortex; in rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep it runs theta with cortex partially disconnected, favouring integration and emotional re-tagging; under stress, encoding of the gist is enhanced and retrieval of detail impaired.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Alzheimer disease begins in the entorhinal cortex and spreads along this exact chain; Korsakoff syndrome destroys the mammillary bodies and anterior thalamus; herpes encephalitis, anoxia and surgical cases such as patient H.M. remove the medial temporal stations; transient global amnesia switches the loop off for hours; post-traumatic stress disorder is, in part, a consolidation disorder of this system.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

Delete this system and the organism becomes a permanent present: intelligence, language, skill and personality survive, but every conversation is met for the first time, which is precisely what H.M. showed for 55 years.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Cortical what-and-where streams via entorhinal cortex; septal acetylcholine and theta pacing; dopaminergic novelty signals; cortisol level.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Event indices via fornix to diencephalon; reinstatement commands via cingulum to cortex; ripple-time replay packets; inhibitory feedback to the stress axis.

• STATE.

◦ Current CA3 attractor; theta phase; encode-versus-retrieve mode flag; consolidation queue of recent indices.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Dentate sparseness 2 to 4 percent; theta 4 to 8 hertz; ripple 100 to 250 hertz; fornix roughly 1.2 million axons per side.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Encoding under 1 second; ripple replay 80 to 250 milliseconds; synaptic consolidation hours; systems consolidation months to years.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Anterograde amnesia (medial temporal or diencephalic), Korsakoff confabulation, Alzheimer disease, transient global amnesia.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

As a engineering pipeline this is the episodic_memory_recorder, subscribing to cortical event features and publishing event indices plus scheduled cortical reinstatements.

Its nearest engineering analogue is a write-ahead log with background compaction: fast one-shot writes to a small store, then slow merging into the large store.

The biological difference is that compaction rewrites the large store's own weights rather than moving records, and the log replays itself only while the machine sleeps.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to encode an event in a fast store, consolidate it into a slow store during sleep, and retrieve it from a partial cue.

Create a list variable called fast store with initial value empty.

Create a list variable called slow store with initial value empty.

Create a number variable called consolidation rate with initial value 0.2.

To encode an event, append the event and its full feature set to the fast store in one step.

To run one night of sleep, perform the following actions for each event in the fast store.

    Replay the event.

    With probability equal to the consolidation rate, copy the event into the slow store.

    Weaken the fast store copy slightly.

End the loop.

To retrieve from a cue, search the fast store and then the slow store for the stored event whose features overlap the cue the most.

If the best overlap exceeds a completion threshold is true, then return the whole event, not just the cued fragment.

Otherwise, report that no memory was found.

The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the encode-sleep-retrieve cycle is a story about data moving between two dictionaries, and Python tells that story in plain sight.

# Encode-store-consolidate-retrieve: complementary learning systems.
# Fast one-shot hippocampal writes, slow interleaved cortical copy
# (Marr 1971; McClelland, McNaughton & O'Reilly, Psych Rev 1995).
import random
random.seed(1)

fast, slow = {}, {}          # hippocampus, neocortex
RATE, THRESH = 0.35, 0.5     # nightly copy chance, completion threshold

def encode(name, features):
    fast[name] = set(features)              # one-shot write

def sleep_night():
    for name, feats in list(fast.items()):  # ripple replay
        if random.random() < RATE:
            slow[name] = set(feats)         # consolidated copy
        if random.random() < 0.15:
            del fast[name]                  # hippocampal fading

def retrieve(cue):
    best, score = None, 0.0
    for store in (fast, slow):
        for name, feats in store.items():
            s = len(feats & cue) / len(feats)
            if s > score:
                best, score = (name, feats), s
    return best if score >= THRESH else None   # pattern completion

encode("beach_day", ["sand", "gull", "kite", "ice_cream"])
encode("exam_day", ["desk", "clock", "pencil", "silence"])
for night in range(10):
    sleep_night()
print("fast holds:", sorted(fast), " slow holds:", sorted(slow))
print("cue {sand, kite} recalls:", retrieve({"sand", "kite"}))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A closed anatomical ring, writable in one shot, erasable by a thumb-sized lesion in any of five different places, and the only memory system that consolidates by replaying itself at ten times life speed while you sleep.


CHAPTER 14. THE SEMANTIC MEMORY SYSTEM (Anterior Temporal Hub-and-Spoke Network) (The Meaning Distillery) (COG)

You know what a canary is without remembering any particular canary.

That trick - knowledge freed from episode - is the product of a hub in the anterior temporal lobes that compresses every sensory and verbal encounter with a thing into one modality-free concept.

Semantic dementia, which dissolves that hub, dissolves the meanings of words, pictures, sounds and smells all together, proving they were stored together.

Meaning names the cargo: concepts, categories, facts about the world.

Distillery names the computation: many episodes in, one essence out, with the particulars boiled away.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The semantic memory system stores general knowledge - concepts, word meanings, facts, object properties - in a hub-and-spoke architecture: modality-specific spoke regions hold what things look, sound, feel and act like, and a transmodal hub in the bilateral anterior temporal lobes binds them into concepts that generalise across modality and context.

Its accomplishment is that similarity in the hub tracks conceptual similarity, not sensory similarity, so a whale sits near a cow, not near a submarine.

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

• The visual ventral-stream areas, the shape-and-colour spoke supplying what things look like.

• The auditory association cortex of the superior temporal lobe, the sound-and-word-form spoke.

• The somatomotor and premotor cortices, the action-and-manipulation spoke encoding how objects are used.

• The orbitofrontal and insular cortices, the valence, taste and smell spoke.

• The bilateral anterior temporal lobes, the ventrolateral and polar temporal cortex, which serve as the transmodal hub distilling all spokes into graded, modality-invariant concept codes.

• The posterior middle temporal gyrus and angular gyrus, secondary convergence zones for events, actions and thematic relations.

• The inferior frontal gyrus, the control station that selects the task-relevant fragment of a concept, the semantic-control component.

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The inferior longitudinal fasciculus carries visual spoke traffic forward to the hub; the middle longitudinal fasciculus and arcuate connect auditory and posterior temporal territory; the uncinate fasciculus joins hub to orbitofrontal valuation and to frontal control; and the anterior commissure plus ventral callosum bind the two hub halves into one redundant, bilateral store, which is why unilateral damage is survivable and bilateral damage is not.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

In each spoke the representation is a modality-specific feature pattern: yellow and small in visual cortex, a song in auditory cortex, perching in motor cortex.

Along the temporal lobe there is a graded convergence, with posterior regions still modality-weighted and the ventrolateral anterior temporal lobe maximally transmodal.

The hub's hand-off adds abstraction: its code responds to canary spoken, written, drawn or heard singing, and its distance metric predicts naming errors in dementia, where canary decays to bird, then to animal, the superordinate skeleton outliving the specific flesh.

Frontal control then adds selection, activating a piano's sound for a listening task and its weight for a moving task.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

The spokes run perceptual feature-map motifs; the hub runs the deep multimodal autoassociator motif, a cross-modal attractor from the microcircuits catalogue; and the frontal station runs the biased-competition selection motif.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Word meaning is available within 200 to 400 milliseconds of hearing a word, indexed by the N400 electrical potential at roughly 400 milliseconds; hub learning itself is slow, requiring interleaved exposure over months to years.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

An educated adult holds roughly 40,000 to 60,000 word lemmas and concept counts of the same order, acquired at about 7 to 10 new words per day through childhood; retrieval runs at conversational speed, two to three concepts per second, with tip-of-the-tongue states marking its retrieval limit.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Cholinergic tone sharpens retrieval precision; the frontal semantic-control loop gates which features reach the workspace; and the hub is notoriously hard to image because it sits in a magnetic-susceptibility shadow, a methodological rather than biological gate that delayed its discovery.

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Hub learning is the slow interleaved learning of the complementary-systems pair: new concepts arrive via the episodic system and are woven in gradually, which protects old structure from catastrophic overwriting.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Spokes mature first; hub abstraction grows through late childhood, and vocabulary continues to expand into the seventh decade, making this the longest-assembling system in the book.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

Convergence cortex at the temporal pole is old primate equipment for cross-modal object knowledge; the human expansion couples it to language, so that verbal labels become one more spoke feeding the same hub.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Thyroid hormone is required for the developmental myelination of the temporal association tracts, and untreated hypothyroid infancy permanently stunts conceptual development; cortisol excess mildly impairs semantic retrieval; otherwise the system's endocrine dependence is indirect.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Awake, the hub serves comprehension continuously; in slow-wave sleep it is the recipient of episodic replay, its weights adjusted toward new knowledge; in rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep, remote semantic associations loosen, which is when distant concepts pair in dreams; under stress, retrieval narrows to dominant meanings.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Semantic dementia, the semantic variant of primary progressive aphasia, atrophies the anterior temporal hub bilaterally and erases concepts across every modality while episodic day-to-day memory survives, the double dissociation with Chapter 13.

Alzheimer disease impairs semantics later and less selectively; herpes encephalitis and temporal-lobe surgery can produce category-specific losses, famously living things versus tools.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

Delete this system and the world becomes unlabelled: objects are seen but not recognised, words are heard as empty sound, and the patient asks, of a picture of a camel, what the strange horse is - perception intact, meaning gone.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Feature patterns from all sensory spokes; word forms from the language system; new episodes from the episodic recorder; frontal task context.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Concept codes to language, valuation, and executive systems; feature reinstatements back down each spoke; the N400 mismatch signal.

• STATE.

◦ Currently active concept attractor; task-selected feature subset; slow weight matrix binding hub to spokes.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Lexicalised concepts roughly 40,000 to 60,000; N400 latency about 400 milliseconds; hub bilaterality redundancy factor 2.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Concept access 200 to 400 milliseconds; new-concept weaving weeks; vocabulary growth decades.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Semantic dementia, transcortical sensory aphasia, category-specific agnosia, anomia.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

As a engineering pipeline this is the semantic_meaning_distillery, subscribing to every perceptual topic and publishing a single concept_code topic consumed by language, valuation and control.

The nearest engineering analogue is a learned embedding layer shared by many input encoders.

The biological difference is that the embedding is bidirectional - the hub can re-ignite each spoke to imagine what it knows - and it is trained only through the episodic system's sleep replay, never by direct gradient access.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to bind modality-specific features into one hub concept and to retrieve any modality from any other through the hub.

Create a table variable called hub with initial value empty, mapping concept names to feature sets.

To learn a concept, perform the following actions.

    Collect the visual features, the sound features, the action features and the verbal label from the spokes.

    Merge all of the collected features into one set.

    Store the merged set in the hub under the concept name.

End the learning step.

To retrieve across modalities, perform the following actions.

    Receive a probe feature set from any single spoke.

    For each concept in the hub, count the overlap between the probe and the stored set.

    Select the concept with the greatest overlap.

    Return the stored features of the selected concept that belong to the requested output modality.

End the retrieval step.

Check if the returned features generalise across the modality of the probe.

Display the result to the console.

The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because a hub is a dictionary of sets and cross-modal retrieval is set intersection, and Python states both in one line each.

# Hub-and-spoke concept binding: a transmodal hub links modality
# spokes so any input modality retrieves any other
# (Patterson, Nestor & Rogers, Nat Rev Neurosci 2007; Lambon Ralph 2017).
hub = {}

def learn(name, **spokes):                    # spokes: modality -> features
    hub[name] = {(m, f) for m, feats in spokes.items() for f in feats}

def retrieve(probe_modality, probe_feats, want_modality):
    probe = {(probe_modality, f) for f in probe_feats}
    best = max(hub, key=lambda n: len(hub[n] & probe))   # hub attractor
    return best, sorted(f for m, f in hub[best] if m == want_modality)

learn("canary", visual=["yellow", "small", "wings"],
      sound=["song"], action=["perches", "flies"], word=["canary"])
learn("camel",  visual=["humped", "large", "tan"],
      sound=["grunt"], action=["walks", "carries"], word=["camel"])

print(retrieve("visual", ["yellow", "wings"], "sound"))  # sight -> song
print(retrieve("word", ["camel"], "visual"))             # name -> look

# Semantic dementia: degrade the hub and specifics go before generals.
hub["canary"] = {t for t in hub["canary"] if t[1] not in ("yellow", "song")}
print("after hub damage:", retrieve("visual", ["yellow", "wings"], "word"))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The only memory store whose contents survive the loss of every episode that built it, housed in a bilateral hub whose focal decay strips the world of meaning one concept at a time, from canary to bird to animal to nothing.


CHAPTER 15. THE PROCEDURAL MEMORY SYSTEM (Basal Ganglia and Cerebellar Skill Loops) (The Habit Forge) (COG)

The amnesic patient H.M. improved at mirror drawing day after day while insisting, each day, that he had never seen the task before.

Knowing-how had survived the total loss of knowing-that, because it lives in a different set of loops: cortex through basal ganglia, and cortex through cerebellum, neither of which needs the hippocampus.

Habit names the product: action sequences compiled until they run without attention.

Forge names the method: skills are not written, they are hammered, thousands of repetitions each adding a small permanent set.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The procedural memory system converts deliberate, feedback-guided action into automatic skill, storing the result as changed gains in cortico-basal-ganglia loops, which learn what to select, and cortico-cerebellar loops, which learn precise timing and calibration.

Its accomplishment is compilation: the same behaviour that once cost the whole executive system runs, after practice, on a fraction of the machinery and none of the awareness.

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

• The sensorimotor and premotor cortices, which propose and execute candidate actions.

• The dorsal striatum, with goal-directed learning in the associative caudate and dorsomedial territory and habit storage shifting to the sensorimotor putamen and dorsolateral territory as practice accumulates.

• The pallidum and the subthalamic nucleus, the selection machinery that releases one action and brakes the rest.

• The substantia nigra pars compacta, whose dopamine stamps in successful selections.

• The motor thalamus, returning the basal-ganglia verdict to cortex.

• The pontine nuclei and cerebellar cortex with the dentate and interposed nuclei, the timing-and-calibration loop that learns forward models of the body.

• The inferior olive, whose climbing fibres deliver the cerebellum's error signals.

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

Corticostriatal projections carry cortical action context to striatum, format massive convergence, roughly 10,000 cortical axons per striatal neuron; the direct and indirect pathways carry go and no-go verdicts through the pallidum; nigrostriatal dopamine carries the reinforcement stamp; and the corticopontocerebellar route, some 20 million axons per side, with return via dentate to thalamus to cortex, closes the calibration loop.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Early in learning the representation is a goal-action-outcome triple held cortically and evaluated step by step.

With repetition the striatum chunks the sequence: task-bracketing activity appears at sequence start and stop, and the middle compresses into a single selected unit.

Control migrates from dorsomedial to dorsolateral striatum, the representation losing its outcome term - which is the definition of habit, action triggered by stimulus regardless of current goal value.

In parallel the cerebellum converts feedback corrections into feedforward prediction, so movement becomes smooth, anticipatory and metrically exact.

The final representation is a stimulus-triggered, dopamine-priced, cerebellum-timed motor program.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

The striatum runs the winner-take-all selection motif and the three-factor reinforcement-learning synapse motif; the cerebellum runs the perceptron-like supervised motif of parallel fibres corrected by climbing fibres; the cortex runs sequence-chunking motifs.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Skill improvement follows a power law over sessions; within-session gains consolidate over roughly six hours and during sleep spindles; cerebellar timing is millisecond-precise while striatal habit formation needs hundreds to thousands of trials over days to weeks.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

Skills are effectively unlimited in number and famously durable over decades, the bicycle effect, but rigid: a compiled habit resists mid-sequence edit, and expert performance degrades when attention is forced back onto it, the choking effect.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Dopamine gates both the learning rate and the vigour of execution, which is why Parkinson disease slows movement and blocks new habit formation at once; stress and cortisol bias control from goal-directed to habitual; the subthalamic brake gates action release globally.

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Corticostriatal synapses learn by dopamine-dependent three-factor rules; cerebellar parallel-fibre synapses undergo long-term depression under climbing-fibre teaching; and both consolidate offline, procedural gains appearing after sleep without further practice.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The loops function from infancy, procedural learning maturing years before episodic memory, which is why children forge motor and grammatical habit early and remember doing so never.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

Basal ganglia selection circuitry is conserved from the lamprey, roughly 500 million years, making this the oldest learning system in Part Three; the cerebellar calibration loop is similarly ancient vertebrate equipment.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Chronic cortisol shifts behavioural control toward habit, an adrenal thumb on this system's scale; thyroid hormone sets cerebellar developmental wiring; sex steroids modulate striatal dopamine sensitivity.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Awake, the system executes and accumulates practice; in slow-wave and stage-two sleep, spindle-linked replay consolidates the day's motor gains; in rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep, sequence integration continues while the muscles are paralysed; under stress, the habit forge takes over from deliberation.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Parkinson disease empties the dopamine stamp and freezes selection; Huntington disease destroys striatal neurons and releases unwanted movement; cerebellar degeneration produces ataxia, the loss of calibration; obsessive-compulsive disorder and addiction are habit-loop pathologies, compiled programs firing against the organism's own goals.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

Delete this system and nothing could ever become easy: every signature, every gearshift, every spoken sentence would remain forever a first attempt performed under full attention.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Cortical action context; nigral dopamine reward stamps; olivary error spikes; proprioceptive state.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Selected action programs via motor thalamus; timing corrections via dentate; vigour settings.

• STATE.

◦ Per-habit strength weights; goal-directed versus habitual control balance; cerebellar forward-model parameters.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Corticostriatal convergence roughly 10,000 to 1; practice to automaticity hundreds to thousands of trials; power-law learning exponent typically 0.2 to 0.6.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Dopamine eligibility window about 1 second; within-session learning minutes; consolidation 6 hours to overnight; retention decades.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Parkinson disease, Huntington disease, cerebellar ataxia, addiction and compulsion as runaway habit.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

As a engineering pipeline this is the procedural_habit_forge, subscribing to action-context and reward-stamp topics and publishing compiled action programs with a per-program strength.

The nearest engineering analogue is a just-in-time compiler with a hot-path cache: interpreted code that is executed often gets compiled and thereafter runs fast and unmonitored.

The biological difference is that compilation is driven by reward rather than call count alone, and the compiled program can outlive the goal that justified it, which no sane compiler permits.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to accumulate habit strength with practice and to hand control from deliberation to habit when the strength crosses a threshold.

Create a number variable called habit strength with initial value 0.

Create a number variable called learning rate with initial value 0.05.

Create a number variable called handover threshold with initial value 0.7.

For each practice trial, perform the following actions.

    Perform the action and observe whether the outcome was rewarded.

    If the outcome was rewarded is true, then perform the following actions.

        Calculate the product of the learning rate and the difference of 1 and the habit strength.

        Add the result to the habit strength.

    Otherwise, reduce the habit strength by a small extinction amount.

    End the conditional block.

End the loop.

Check if the habit strength is greater than the handover threshold.

If the check is true, then route control to the fast habitual pathway, ignoring the current goal value.

Otherwise, route control to the slow goal-directed pathway.

Display the habit strength and the controlling pathway to the console.

The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because a saturating strength update and a control handover are three lines of arithmetic and one comparison, and Python leaves them unobscured.

# Habit strength accumulation and the goal-to-habit handover.
# Reward stamps strengthen a stimulus-response link until it
# controls behaviour outcome-blind (Yin & Knowlton, Nat Rev
# Neurosci 2006; Graybiel, Annu Rev Neurosci 2008).
ALPHA, EXTINCT, HANDOVER = 0.05, 0.02, 0.7

strength, log = 0.0, []
rewarded = [True] * 120 + [False] * 30      # then the reward is devalued

for t, r in enumerate(rewarded, 1):
    if r:
        strength += ALPHA * (1.0 - strength)     # saturating growth
    else:
        strength = max(0.0, strength - EXTINCT)  # slow extinction
    controller = "HABIT (putamen)" if strength > HANDOVER \
                 else "GOAL (caudate + cortex)"
    if t in (1, 30, 60, 120, 150):
        log.append((t, round(strength, 2), controller))

for t, s, c in log:
    print(f"trial {t:3d}  strength {s:.2f}  control: {c}")
# Note: after devaluation the habit keeps control for many trials -
# the signature of habitual behaviour, and of addiction.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The memory system with no access to consciousness and no need of the hippocampus, storing its knowledge as gains in two ancient motor loops, provable in an amnesic who gets better every day at a task he swears he has never done.


CHAPTER 16. THE WORKING MEMORY SYSTEM (Prefrontal-Parietal Persistent-Activity Circuit) (The Present-Thought Blackboard) (COG)

Hold a phone number in mind while finding a pen and you are running the most studied circuit in cognitive neuroscience: prefrontal and parietal neurons firing persistently, for seconds, about something that is no longer there.

Nothing is written to a synapse; the memory is the activity, and when the activity stops, the number is gone.

Present names the tense: this store holds only the current few seconds of thought.

Thought names the clientele: every reasoning step rents space here.

Blackboard names the mechanism: a small erasable surface, written by attention, read by everything, wiped by the next task.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The working memory system maintains and manipulates a handful of items in an active, immediately usable state after the stimulus is gone, using recurrent excitation in prefrontal-parietal circuitry supported by the mediodorsal thalamus, with a striatal-dopaminergic gate deciding what may enter.

Its accomplishment is the decoupling of thought from stimulus: comparison, arithmetic, planning and sentence understanding all require holding one thing while processing another.

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

• The sensory association cortices, which supply candidate contents in their native codes.

• The posterior parietal cortex, the intraparietal territory whose activity tracks the number of items held and best predicts individual capacity.

• The dorsolateral prefrontal cortex, the classic delay-activity station, holding items robustly against distraction and applying manipulation.

• The mediodorsal thalamus, which sustains and amplifies prefrontal delay activity; silencing it collapses the memory.

• The basal ganglia input gate, striatum and pallidum deciding, under dopamine, which items are admitted or updated.

• The frontal eye fields and premotor cortex, where held content is formatted for action.

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The superior longitudinal fasciculus binds prefrontal and parietal stations bidirectionally, cargo item codes and attentional pointers; thalamocortical loops with the mediodorsal nucleus carry sustaining drive; and pallido-thalamic disinhibition implements the gate's open command.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

In sensory cortex the candidate is a stimulus-locked pattern that decays in hundreds of milliseconds.

Admission through the gate converts it into an attractor state: recurrent N-methyl-D-aspartate (NMDA) receptor mediated excitation among similarly tuned prefrontal neurons, sculpted by lateral inhibition, keeps a bump of activity alive across the delay.

Parietal cortex holds a more stimulus-faithful copy while prefrontal cortex holds a more task-transformed one, and manipulation - reordering, arithmetic - is a rotation of the prefrontal code that the parietal copy does not undergo.

The read-out hand-off reformats the surviving attractor into motor or linguistic coordinates.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

The core is the continuous and discrete attractor motif of the microcircuits catalogue; the gate runs the striatal disinhibition motif; capacity limits emerge from the lateral-inhibition normalisation motif, bumps competing for a shared surface.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Delay activity spans roughly 1 to 30 seconds; unrehearsed content fades in about 2 to 20 seconds; gamma-band bursts riding on theta phases carry item packets, and activity-silent intervals bridged by short-term synaptic facilitation of tens to hundreds of milliseconds patch the gaps between bursts.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

Capacity is roughly 4 items, range 3 to 5, for visual arrays, with the older 7 plus or minus 2 figure for digits reflecting rehearsal and chunking; precision degrades as items are added, supporting shared-resource accounts; individual capacity correlates about 0.6 to 0.8 with fluid intelligence measures.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Dopamine acting on D1 receptors follows an inverted-U: too little and attractors cannot hold, too much and they cannot update, which is why both stress and Parkinson medication can impair this system; acetylcholine and noradrenaline set robustness to distraction; the basal-ganglia gate is the explicit admission control.

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

The contents are not learned at all - that is the point - but the gating policy is, by striatal reinforcement learning, and capacity-adjacent skills improve with training even though raw capacity barely does.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Capacity grows from about 1 to 2 items in toddlerhood to the adult 4 by adolescence, tracking prefrontal myelination, and declines measurably from the sixth decade.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

Delay activity exists in monkey, rat and even insect central complexes; the human expansion is in the manipulation and gating machinery, not the raw store.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Cortisol at moderate acute levels sharpens and at high levels floods the dopamine inverted-U; oestrogen modulates prefrontal dopamine and measurably shifts working-memory performance across the cycle; thyroid excess produces the distractibility of an ungated blackboard.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Awake, the blackboard runs continuously; in slow-wave sleep it is erased, delay activity being incompatible with the slow oscillation's silent states; in rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep, fragments ignite without gate control, one account of dream incoherence; under acute stress the gate loosens and capacity effectively drops.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Schizophrenia shows reduced dorsolateral prefrontal delay activity and spine loss on the very layer 3 pyramidal cells that hold the attractors; attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder is in part a gating disorder; delirium is the blackboard failing globally; prefrontal lesions produce distractibility and perseveration.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

Delete this system and the organism becomes purely reactive: perception and long-term memory survive, but no two ideas can be held at once, so comparison, plan and sentence all die in the same instant.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Candidate items from sensory and semantic systems; gate verdicts from the striatum; dopamine, noradrenaline and acetylcholine tone; task rules from executive control.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Held items to every consumer system on demand; attentional pointers back to sensory cortex; formatted content to motor and language systems.

• STATE.

◦ The 0 to about 4 active attractors with their precisions; gate open-or-closed status; current rehearsal schedule.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Capacity 3 to 5 items; D1 dopamine inverted-U optimum; NMDA-receptor decay about 100 milliseconds as the recurrence glue.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Item admission 100 to 300 milliseconds; unrehearsed decay 2 to 20 seconds; facilitation bridges 100 to 500 milliseconds; wipe on task switch under 1 second.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Schizophrenia delay-activity deficit, attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder, delirium, dysexecutive distractibility.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

As a engineering pipeline this is the working_memory_blackboard, a small stateful module subscribing to a gated admission topic and publishing its slots to all readers.

The nearest engineering analogue is a register file with admission control, or a bounded in-memory cache with eviction.

The biological difference is that the registers are self-refreshing attractors that pay a continuous energy bill and can be corrupted by their neighbours, since all slots share one recurrent surface.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to maintain a small number of items as self-refreshing slots, admit new items only through a gate, and evict the weakest item when the surface is full.

Create a list variable called slots with initial value empty.

Create a number variable called capacity with initial value 4.

Create a number variable called decay with initial value 0.05.

On each time step, perform the following actions.

    For each item in the slots, reduce its activation by the decay amount.

    If an item's activation falls below 0.2 is true, then remove the item, because the attractor has collapsed.

    If a rehearsal signal targets an item is true, then restore that item's activation to 1.

End the time step.

When a new item arrives, perform the following actions.

    If the gate is closed is true, then discard the item.

    Else if the slots hold fewer items than the capacity is true, then admit the item with activation 1.

    Otherwise, remove the item with the lowest activation and admit the new item.

End the admission step.

Display the surviving slots to the console.

The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because slots, decay, gating and eviction are a list of pairs and four short rules, and Python keeps the rules as legible as the prose.

# Limited-slot maintenance with dopamine-style gating.
# Items persist as decaying attractors; a gate controls admission;
# a full surface evicts the weakest (Miller & Cohen 2001;
# O'Reilly & Frank, Neural Comput 2006; Luck & Vogel 1997: ~4 slots).
CAPACITY, DECAY, COLLAPSE = 4, 0.05, 0.2
slots = {}                                   # item -> activation

def step(rehearse=None):
    for item in list(slots):
        slots[item] -= DECAY                 # attractor leakage
        if item == rehearse:
            slots[item] = 1.0                # attentional refresh
        if slots[item] < COLLAPSE:
            del slots[item]                  # bump collapses

def admit(item, gate_open):
    if not gate_open:
        return                               # distractor rejected
    if len(slots) >= CAPACITY:
        del slots[min(slots, key=slots.get)] # evict weakest
    slots[item] = 1.0

for item in ["7", "4", "1", "9"]:
    admit(item, gate_open=True)
admit("advert_jingle", gate_open=False)      # gate blocks distraction
for t in range(12):
    step(rehearse="7" if t % 3 == 0 else None)
admit("5", gate_open=True)                   # forces an eviction
print({k: round(v, 2) for k, v in slots.items()})

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The only memory in the book that exists as ongoing electricity rather than changed synapses, holding about four things for about ten seconds at a continuous metabolic price, and vanishing entirely the moment its neurons fall silent.


CHAPTER 17. THE REWARD AND MOTIVATION SYSTEM (Mesolimbic Dopamine Valuation Circuit) (The Wanting Economy) (COG)

Every option the brain ever considers passes through a pricing bureau of perhaps half a million dopamine neurons.

When the world pays out more than predicted they burst, when it pays out as predicted they hold steady, and when it pays out less they fall silent - a signed teaching signal that Wolfram Schultz's recordings matched, line for line, to the temporal-difference algorithm of machine learning.

Wanting names the product, deliberately distinct from liking: this system energises pursuit, and can drive pursuit of things no longer even enjoyed.

Economy names the scope: one common currency, spent on everything from sugar to status.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The reward and motivation system computes the value of predicted outcomes, broadcasts a reward prediction error - received minus expected - as phasic dopamine, and uses that error to update stored values and to set the vigour of ongoing behaviour.

Its accomplishment is that a single scalar teaching signal, applied to whatever synapses were recently active, lets the entire forebrain learn from consequence.

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

• The lateral hypothalamus and pedunculopontine inputs, reporting physiological need and sensory reward.

• The ventral tegmental area, the midbrain dopamine source computing and broadcasting the prediction error.

• The lateral habenula, the disappointment station, driving dopamine pauses through the rostromedial tegmentum when outcomes fall short.

• The nucleus accumbens, core and shell, the ventral striatal ledger where cue values are written and wanting is energised.

• The ventral pallidum, the hedonic and output relay of the ventral loop.

• The orbitofrontal cortex, holding a flexible, model-based menu of outcome values.

• The anterior cingulate cortex, pricing effort against payoff.

• The amygdala, attaching value to cues and states, especially aversive ones.

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The medial forebrain bundle carries ventral tegmental dopamine axons to accumbens, prefrontal cortex and amygdala, cargo a broadcast scalar in spikes per second; corticostriatal projections from orbitofrontal and cingulate cortex carry predicted values to the accumbens; the habenulo-tegmental route carries negative surprises; accumbens to pallidum to thalamus closes the ventral selection loop.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Hypothalamic and sensory stations represent raw utility: sweetness, relief, warmth.

The orbitofrontal station converts it into expected value per option, sensitive to current state, so that food value collapses when sated.

The ventral tegmental area subtracts expectation from outcome: unexpected reward gives a burst of roughly 100 to 200 milliseconds, fully predicted reward gives nothing, and omission gives a pause - and with training the burst migrates backward in time from the reward to its earliest reliable predictor.

In the accumbens the broadcast error gates plasticity at recently active corticostriatal synapses, writing updated values, while tonic dopamine level separately scales response vigour.

The hand-off to the dorsal loops of Chapter 15 turns persistent values into persistent habits, the spiral from wanting to doing.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

The core motif is the three-factor dopamine-gated synapse; the habenula-tegmentum pair runs a sign-inversion motif; the accumbens runs the same winner-take-all selection motif as the dorsal striatum; the orbitofrontal station runs a lookup-table valuation motif.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Phasic bursts and pauses last 100 to 500 milliseconds with latencies near 100 milliseconds; the eligibility window pairing dopamine with recent synaptic activity is roughly 1 second; tonic dopamine shifts over minutes set motivational vigour; value learning itself spans trials to years.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

There are only about 400,000 to 600,000 dopamine neurons in the human midbrain, versus 86 billion neurons total, an extreme broadcast bottleneck; the signal is one signed scalar, so the system cannot say what was better, only that something was; discounting makes delayed rewards lose value with delay, hyperbolically in behaviour.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Hunger, thirst and hormonal state retune values at the hypothalamic and orbitofrontal stations; stress and cortisol sensitise the system to drugs and relapse; opioid hedonic hotspots in accumbens shell and ventral pallidum gate liking separately from dopamine wanting.

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

This system is plasticity: its whole output is a learning signal, and its own corticostriatal synapses learn by the very dopamine rule they broadcast, which is what addiction exploits with pharmacological prediction errors that never converge to zero.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Dopamine wiring matures through adolescence, when reward sensitivity peaks before prefrontal restraint arrives, a schedule that explains much teenage actuarial data.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

Dopamine reward learning is conserved from insects and molluscs onward; the vertebrate elaboration adds cortical model-based valuation atop an invertebrate-grade error broadcast.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Ghrelin and leptin retune food value in opposite directions at hypothalamus and ventral tegmentum; cortisol sensitises the loop; sex steroids modulate accumbens dopamine; and the system drives the endocrine axes in return, since wanting mobilises the body.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Awake, the economy prices continuously; in slow-wave sleep, accumbens participates in replay that preferentially consolidates rewarded experience; in rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep, dopamine tone persists while noradrenaline vanishes, one reason dreams are motivated but unfocused; under stress, valuation shifts toward immediate reward.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Addiction is this system taught by chemically guaranteed prediction errors; Parkinson disease removes its dorsal cousin and, in treatment, can flood this one into impulse-control disorders; anhedonia in depression is a pricing failure; pathological gambling rides variable-ratio prediction errors that never settle.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

Delete this system and the organism knows everything and wants nothing: dopamine-depleted animals will starve beside food they will eat if it is placed in the mouth, liking intact, pursuit abolished.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Outcome reports from sensory and visceral systems; predicted values from orbitofrontal cortex; disappointment from lateral habenula; need states from hypothalamus; cortisol and gut hormones.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Phasic reward prediction error, a signed scalar, broadcast forebrain-wide; tonic vigour level; updated cue values to the selection loops.

• STATE.

◦ The value table over cues and options; current tonic dopamine; satiety-adjusted utilities.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Dopamine neuron count roughly 0.5 million; burst latency about 100 milliseconds; eligibility window about 1 second; learning rate behaviourally 0.05 to 0.3.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Phasic signal 100 to 500 milliseconds; tonic shifts minutes; value learning trials to years; addiction sensitisation months to permanent.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Addiction, anhedonic depression, impulse-control disorder under dopaminergic therapy, apathy after ventral pallidal lesions.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

As a engineering pipeline this is the reward_teaching_system, subscribing to outcome and prediction topics and publishing a single broadcast error topic that every learning module consumes as its learning-rate-times-sign input.

The nearest engineering analogue is the temporal-difference critic of an actor-critic reinforcement learner.

The biological difference is that the critic's output is a diffuse chemical, delivered by volume to whoever was recently active rather than addressed to named parameters, and it simultaneously sets the speed of the actor it teaches.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to learn the value of a cue by repeatedly comparing received reward with predicted reward and nudging the prediction by the error.

Create a number variable called predicted value with initial value 0.

Create a number variable called learning rate with initial value 0.1.

For each trial, perform the following actions.

    Present the cue and then deliver the actual reward.

    Calculate the difference of the actual reward and the predicted value.

    Name the result the prediction error.

    Calculate the product of the learning rate and the prediction error.

    Add the result to the predicted value.

    If the prediction error is greater than 0 is true, then emit a dopamine burst proportional to the error.

    Else if the prediction error is less than 0 is true, then emit a dopamine pause proportional to the error.

    Otherwise, emit no phasic signal, because the world matched the forecast.

    End the conditional block.

End the loop.

Display the final predicted value and the final prediction error to the console.

The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the entire theory is one subtraction and one update per trial, and Python lets the two lines carry the chapter.

# Temporal-difference reward prediction error: burst to surprise,
# silence to the expected, pause to omission, and backward transfer
# of the burst from reward to cue (Schultz, Dayan & Montague,
# Science 1997; Sutton & Barto TD learning).
ALPHA, GAMMA = 0.15, 0.95
V = [0.0] * 6                      # value at 6 time steps; cue at t=1
REWARD_T, R = 5, 1.0               # juice arrives at t=5

def trial(omit=False):
    deltas = []
    for t in range(5):
        r = R if (t + 1 == REWARD_T and not omit) else 0.0
        delta = r + GAMMA * V[t + 1] - V[t]      # the TD error
        V[t] += ALPHA * delta                    # dopamine teaches
        deltas.append(round(delta, 2))
    return deltas

for n in range(200):
    d = trial()
    if n in (0, 20, 199):
        print(f"trial {n+1:3d}  dopamine per step: {d}")

print("omission  dopamine per step:", trial(omit=True))
# Early: burst at reward time. Late: burst at the cue, nothing at
# reward. Omission after training: a pause (negative error) at the
# moment the juice should have arrived.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Half a million neurons implementing, cell for cell and sign for sign, an algorithm later derived independently by computer scientists, and holding the price list against which every other system in this book must bid.


CHAPTER 18. THE LANGUAGE SYSTEM (Perisylvian Dual-Stream Network) (The Word Loom) (COG)

Around the Sylvian fissure of the left hemisphere, and only there, and only in our species in this form, sits the machinery that turns air pressure into meaning and intention into sequenced breath.

The nineteenth-century pair of Broca's and Wernicke's territories has become a dual-stream architecture: a ventral stream weaving sound into sense, a dorsal stream weaving sound into articulation.

Word names the unit: a learned pairing of form and meaning, tens of thousands strong.

Loom names the work: words are not stored as sentences; sentences are woven on demand, thread over thread, at three per second, by rules no speaker can state.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The language system maps between structured sound or sign and structured meaning in both directions - comprehension and production - through a left-lateralised perisylvian network organised as two streams: a ventral sound-to-meaning stream and a dorsal sound-to-articulation stream, per the Hickok and Poeppel model.

Its accomplishment is discrete infinity: a finite store of words and rules yielding an unbounded set of expressible thoughts.

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

• The superior temporal auditory cortex, extracting the phonemic code from sound within its first 100 milliseconds.

• The middle and posterior temporal cortex, the lexical interface where word forms contact the semantic hub of Chapter 14.

• The anterior temporal and inferior frontal ventral-stream stations, composing word meanings into phrase and sentence meaning.

• The temporoparietal junction area Spt, the sensorimotor interface translating auditory targets into articulatory coordinates.

• The inferior frontal gyrus, Broca's territory, handling syntactic sequencing, selection among competing words, and speech planning.

• The ventral premotor and motor cortex, executing the articulatory score across roughly 100 muscles.

• The basal ganglia and cerebellar loops of Chapter 15, which house the procedural grammar-and-fluency machinery.

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The arcuate fasciculus with the superior longitudinal fascicle forms the dorsal stream, origin posterior superior temporal cortex, destination premotor and inferior frontal cortex, cargo phonological sequences for repetition and production; its disconnection yields conduction aphasia, fluent speech with broken repetition.

The inferior fronto-occipital, uncinate and inferior longitudinal fasciculi plus the extreme capsule form the ventral stream, cargo sound-to-meaning mappings.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Sound arrives as a spectrotemporal pattern and is recoded within about 100 milliseconds into phonemic categories, discarding speaker detail.

The lexical interface matches phoneme sequences against roughly 40,000 to 60,000 stored word forms, resolving competition in about a quarter of a second, cohort by cohort.

The ventral stream's hand-off adds combination: meanings compose into predicate-argument structure, with the N400 potential at about 400 milliseconds indexing semantic fit and the P600 at about 600 milliseconds indexing syntactic repair.

Production runs the loom in reverse: a message becomes a syntactic frame, the frame is filled with retrieved lemmas, the lemmas are spelled into phonology, and area Spt converts the phonological score into motor coordinates, with an internal forward-model copy checking the output before and after the lips move.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

The temporal stations run competitive lexical-selection motifs; the frontal station runs sequence-generation and selection motifs; the dorsal interface runs the forward-model comparator motif; nested oscillations, theta for syllables at 4 to 8 hertz and gamma for phonemes, run the parsing clock.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Comprehension is incremental at 2 to 3 words per second with commitments made within 200 to 400 milliseconds per word; production plans roughly a clause ahead; conversational turn-taking gaps average a remarkable 200 milliseconds, requiring prediction of the partner's ending.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

Vocabulary 40,000 to 60,000 lemmas; speech rate 120 to 180 words per minute; phonological short-term store about 2 seconds of inner speech; centre-embedded clauses fail beyond depth two, a working-memory limit, not a grammatical one.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Arousal systems set fluency; the basal-ganglia gate meters speech initiation, which is why Parkinson disease quiets speech and why stuttering implicates this loop; in about 95 percent of right-handers and about 70 percent of left-handers the left hemisphere holds the loom.

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Word learning continues lifelong via the episodic-then-semantic route; phonology and grammar have sensitive periods closing through adolescence, after which new languages carry accents and effortful syntax; early left-hemisphere injury can move the whole system rightward, an option that closes in childhood.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Babbling at 6 months, first words near 12 months, a vocabulary explosion after 18 months, grammar essentially adult by age four: the fastest acquisition of any system in Part Three, on a schedule so uniform it is plainly maturational.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

The dorsal auditory-motor loop is elaborated from primate circuitry, dramatically strengthened in the human arcuate; the FOXP2 story and the absence of any animal equivalent of open-ended syntax mark this as the youngest system in the book, plausibly under 200,000 years old in present form.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Thyroid hormone is required for perisylvian myelination and untreated congenital deficiency devastates language; sex steroids contribute to the modest average female advantage in verbal fluency; otherwise the coupling is indirect, through arousal and development.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Awake, the loom runs both directions continuously, including as inner speech; in slow-wave sleep, newly learned words are consolidated into the lexicon, gaining competition effects overnight; in rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep, the loom produces fluent but unmonitored dream speech; under stress, retrieval blocks and disfluencies multiply.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Broca aphasia breaks the frame, telegraphic effortful speech with comprehension largely spared; Wernicke aphasia breaks the lexical interface, fluent empty speech with broken comprehension; conduction aphasia cuts the dorsal cable; the primary progressive aphasias dissolve stations selectively; developmental dyslexia and stuttering are assembly disorders of the mapping and metering machinery.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

This system makes the species: every other system in the book can only be described, taught, and accumulated across generations because this one exists, and its loss - aphasia - isolates a fully intact mind behind a failed interface.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Phonemic streams from auditory cortex; visual word forms from the reading route; concept codes from the semantic hub; messages from the executive and social systems.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Meaning structures to the semantic and workspace systems; articulatory scores to motor cortex; inner speech to working memory's phonological loop.

• STATE.

◦ Current parse tree and cohort; the production pipeline's clause buffer; forward-model prediction of one's own voice.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Lexicon 40,000 to 60,000; speech rate 120 to 180 words per minute; N400 at about 400 and P600 at about 600 milliseconds; left lateralisation about 95 percent in right-handers.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Phoneme categorisation about 100 milliseconds; word recognition 200 to 400 milliseconds; turn-taking gap about 200 milliseconds; sensitive-period closure over years.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Broca, Wernicke and conduction aphasias, primary progressive aphasia, dyslexia, stuttering.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

As a engineering pipeline this is the language_word_loom, subscribing to phoneme and concept topics and publishing meaning structures and articulatory scores through paired comprehension and production sub-pipelines that share one lexicon.

The nearest engineering analogue is a bidirectional transducer, a parser and generator compiled from one grammar.

The biological difference is that the grammar was never written down anywhere in the system, existing only as the statistics of its connections, and the parser starts emitting interpretations before the input ends.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to run one turn of the comprehension-production loop, mapping heard words to a meaning and the replied meaning back to words.

Create a table variable called lexicon with initial value empty, mapping word forms to meanings and meanings to word forms.

To comprehend, perform the following actions.

    For each incoming word form, look up its meaning in the lexicon as soon as the word is recognised, without waiting for the sentence to end.

    Compose the meanings in order into one message structure of the form actor, action, object.

End the comprehension step.

To produce a reply, perform the following actions.

    Take the reply message structure from the thinking systems.

    For each element of the message, look up its word form in the lexicon.

    Arrange the word forms according to the word-order rule of the language.

    Send the arranged word forms to the articulation stage.

End the production step.

Check if comprehending one's own produced sentence returns the original reply message.

Display the result of the round-trip check to the console.

The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because a two-way lexicon is a pair of dictionaries and the round-trip test is one equality, and Python makes the loop's symmetry visible.

# Comprehension-production loop over a shared lexicon: the dual
# streams as one bidirectional transducer, verified by round-trip
# (Hickok & Poeppel, Nat Rev Neurosci 2007; Levelt's production model).
to_meaning = {"the": None, "dog": "DOG", "girl": "GIRL",
              "sees": "SEE", "feeds": "FEED"}
to_word = {v: k for k, v in to_meaning.items() if v}

def comprehend(words):                        # ventral stream
    roles = [to_meaning[w] for w in words if to_meaning.get(w)]
    return {"actor": roles[0], "action": roles[1], "object": roles[2]}

def produce(message):                         # dorsal stream, reversed
    frame = [message["actor"], message["action"], message["object"]]
    words = []
    for meaning in frame:                     # lemma retrieval
        words += ["the", to_word[meaning]] if meaning != frame[1] \
                 else [to_word[meaning]]      # crude syntax frame
    return words

heard = ["the", "girl", "sees", "the", "dog"]
msg = comprehend(heard)
reply = {"actor": "GIRL", "action": "FEED", "object": "DOG"}
spoken = produce(reply)
print("understood:", msg)
print("spoken    :", " ".join(spoken))
print("round trip ok:", comprehend(spoken) == reply)  # self-monitoring

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A left-perisylvian machine found in one species, weaving an infinite set of sentences from a finite lexicon at three words per second, and capable of the strangest dissociation in neurology: fluent, grammatical, perfectly articulated speech that means nothing at all.


CHAPTER 19. THE EXECUTIVE CONTROL SYSTEM (Frontoparietal Cognitive-Control Network) (The Cognitive Government) (COG)

Somebody has to decide which of the other systems runs.

Somebody has to hold the goal, notice when habit answers where thought was needed, pay the switch cost of changing task, and veto the prepotent response - and that somebody is a lateral prefrontal, cingulate and parietal coalition that produces no perception, no memory and no movement of its own.

Cognitive names its jurisdiction: it governs processing, not muscles.

Government names its method: it does not do the work; it sets rules, allocates resources, monitors outcomes, and levies the taxes known as effort.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The executive control system maintains goals and task rules, biases the competitions running in every other system so that the goal-relevant pathway wins, monitors performance for conflict and error, and reconfigures the whole cognitive economy when the task changes.

Its accomplishment is behaviour governed by intention rather than by the strongest stimulus-response habit, which is what tests such as the Stroop task measure by putting the two into direct conflict.

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

• The dorsolateral prefrontal cortex, holding the active task set - the current mapping from situations to responses - and biasing posterior systems accordingly.

• The anterior cingulate and adjacent medial frontal cortex, the monitor, detecting conflict and error - its error-related negativity fires within 100 milliseconds of a mistake - and pricing effort.

• The right inferior frontal gyrus with the pre-supplementary motor area, the braking station for outright inhibition of action and thought.

• The frontopolar cortex, stacking goals, holding the postponed task while a subtask runs.

• The posterior parietal cortex, implementing the attentional settings the prefrontal rules demand.

• The mediodorsal thalamus and the basal-ganglia loops, gating which task set occupies the working-memory blackboard of Chapter 16.

• The locus coeruleus interface, through which the monitor adjusts global gain.

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The superior longitudinal fasciculus carries rule-and-attention traffic between frontal and parietal stations; the cingulum carries monitoring traffic; frontostriatal loops carry task-set gating; a cingulate-to-locus-coeruleus projection carries the demand for more gain; and a hyperdirect cortico-subthalamic route, cargo stop commands, implements the brake in roughly 100 to 200 milliseconds.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

The representation entering the system is a goal, an abstract desired state from valuation and social systems.

The dorsolateral station transforms it into a task set, a concrete rule table - if colour task, map ink colour to names - held as patterns that physically bias posterior pathways, Miller and Cohen's guided activation.

The cingulate station transforms performance into evaluation: measured conflict between co-active responses, detected errors, and a running estimate of whether control is worth its cost.

The hand-off back to dorsolateral cortex closes the control loop, evaluation adjusting rule strength, and the frontopolar station adds the stack, so that one government can administer nested tasks without losing the outer one.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

The core is the biased-competition motif applied system-wide; the cingulate runs a comparator-and-cost motif; the braking station runs the hyperdirect global-interrupt motif; task sets themselves live on the attractor motifs of Chapter 16's circuitry.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Rule application adds tens of milliseconds per decision; task switching costs 100 to 300 milliseconds and rises with set complexity; the error signal appears inside 100 milliseconds; sustained control fatigues over tens of minutes, whatever the mechanism of that fatigue proves to be.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

One task set can govern at a time, so genuine multitasking is rapid alternation paying full switch costs; the goal stack manages perhaps two to three levels reliably; control is effortful and is experienced as costly, so the system economises, delegating to habit whenever the habit is good enough.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Prefrontal dopamine stabilises task sets on the Chapter 16 inverted-U; noradrenaline from the locus coeruleus trades focused exploitation against distractible exploration; acute stress shifts control from this system to the habit forge, which is why practised routines survive emergencies and novel plans do not.

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

The system learns abstract rules and strategies rapidly, one instruction sufficing where other systems need trials; what it automates it hands down to Chapter 15, so its own workload is perpetually being exported.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Last to mature: prefrontal myelination and synaptic pruning run into the mid-twenties, and executive measures track that schedule, which is why insurance actuaries and neuroscientists agree about age 25.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

Granular lateral prefrontal cortex is a primate invention, vastly expanded in humans; the cingulate monitor is older mammalian equipment; the combination - abstract rules governing a conserved motivational core - is the book's best candidate for what most distinguishes human cognition after language.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Cortisol is this system's chief antagonist, acute elevations impairing prefrontal function while sparing the amygdala and striatum; thyroid disorder in either direction presents as executive complaint; glucose availability constrains sustained control.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Awake, the government sits continuously, its authority sagging with time-on-task; in slow-wave sleep it is largely offline, one reason sleep deprivation presents first as executive failure; in rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep the dorsolateral station is deactivated, and dreams are correspondingly ungoverned; under stress, power devolves to habit and amygdala.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

The dysexecutive syndrome after frontal injury - the patient Phineas Gage being the founding case - leaves intelligence testable but life unmanageable; attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder, schizophrenia, depression and obsessive-compulsive disorder all carry executive signatures; frontotemporal dementia can dissolve the government while memory stands.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

Delete this system and every other chapter still runs, but nothing coordinates them: behaviour becomes a chain of environmentally triggered habits and impulses, coherent for seconds and incoherent across the day - which is a fair clinical description of the dysexecutive patient.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Goals and prices from the reward system; conflict and error telemetry from its own monitor; state reports from all governed systems; cortisol and arousal levels.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Task-set bias to every processing system; stop commands via the hyperdirect route; gain requests to the locus coeruleus; effort prices back to valuation.

• STATE.

◦ Active task set; goal stack contents; running conflict and error estimates; fatigue level.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Switch cost 100 to 300 milliseconds; error signal under 100 milliseconds; stop latency 100 to 200 milliseconds; goal stack depth 2 to 3.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Rule installation under 1 second from instruction; set stability seconds to hours; vigilance decrement over tens of minutes; maturation to the mid-twenties.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Dysexecutive syndrome, attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder, perseveration, disinhibition, frontotemporal dementia.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

As a engineering pipeline this is the executive_control_governor, subscribing to goal and telemetry topics and publishing bias, stop and gain-request messages to every other pipeline.

The nearest engineering analogue is an operating-system scheduler with an interrupt controller.

The biological difference is that the governor cannot pre-empt by fiat: it can only bias competitions it does not own, so a strong enough habit or stimulus can outvote the government, a failure mode operating systems are specifically designed to exclude.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to hold a task set, apply it to incoming stimuli, and pay a switch cost including an inhibition penalty when the task changes.

Create a text variable called active task with initial value empty.

Create a number variable called base time with initial value 500 milliseconds.

Create a number variable called switch cost with initial value 200 milliseconds.

Create a number variable called inhibition cost with initial value 100 milliseconds.

For each trial, perform the following actions.

    Receive the cued task and the stimulus.

    If the cued task differs from the active task is true, then perform the following actions.

        Add the switch cost to the trial's response time, for installing the new rule table.

        Add the inhibition cost to the trial's response time, for suppressing the old rule table.

        Set the active task to the cued task.

    End the conditional block.

    Apply the active task's rule table to the stimulus to choose the response.

    If the stimulus also affords the competing task's response is true, then add a conflict penalty and notify the monitor.

End the loop.

Display each trial's response and response time to the console.

The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because task sets are dictionaries, switching is a comparison, and the costs are additions, so the whole psychology of task switching fits in one loop.

# Task-set switching with inhibition cost: repeat trials are fast,
# switch trials pay to install the new set and suppress the old
# (Monsell, Trends Cogn Sci 2003; Miller & Cohen, Annu Rev 2001).
TASKS = {"colour": {"RED_word_GREEN_ink": "green"},
         "word":   {"RED_word_GREEN_ink": "red"}}
BASE, SWITCH, INHIBIT, CONFLICT = 500, 200, 100, 80   # milliseconds

active = None
trials = [("colour",), ("colour",), ("word",), ("word",),
          ("colour",), ("word",)]

for cued, in trials:
    rt = BASE
    if cued != active:
        rt += SWITCH + INHIBIT      # reconfigure + suppress old set
        active = cued
    stimulus = "RED_word_GREEN_ink"
    response = TASKS[active][stimulus]
    rt += CONFLICT                  # Stroop stimulus affords both tasks
    print(f"task {active:6s}  respond '{response:5s}'  RT {rt} ms")
# Repeat trials: 580 ms. Switch trials: 880 ms. The 300 ms gap is
# the measurable price of changing the mind's government.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The only system whose entire output is the reconfiguration of other systems, measurable as a few hundred milliseconds of switch cost, maturing last, failing first under stress and sleep loss, and leaving, when lesioned, a patient whose every faculty tests normal and whose life nonetheless falls apart.


CHAPTER 20. THE SOCIAL COGNITION SYSTEM (Mentalising and Face-Intention Network) (The Other-Minds Mirror) (COG)

Watch a hand reach for the last biscuit and you know, instantly and without instruction, that its owner wants it, believes it is there, and may feel guilt.

None of that - want, belief, guilt - is visible; it is computed, by a dedicated coalition of temporoparietal, temporal, medial prefrontal and amygdalar stations that treats other agents as the single most important objects in the world.

Other-minds names the cargo: representations of what other agents perceive, intend, believe and feel, including beliefs you know to be false.

Mirror names the method: the system models others partly by running its own machinery in simulation, self as the first draft of everyone.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The social cognition system detects agents, reads faces, gazes and biological motion, infers goals and intentions from action, attributes beliefs and desires - the capacity called theory of mind or mentalising - and computes social emotions and norms.

Its accomplishment is the false-belief computation: representing a world-state and, simultaneously, another agent's incorrect model of that world-state, and predicting behaviour from the model rather than the world.

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

• The fusiform face area of the ventral temporal cortex, the face-identity specialist.

• The posterior superior temporal sulcus, reading gaze direction, expression dynamics and biological motion, the verbs of the social world.

• The temporoparietal junction, especially the right, the belief-attribution station, selectively engaged when reasoning about others' mental states and perspectives.

• The amygdala, tagging faces and social events with significance and threat.

• The anterior insula and anterior cingulate, the empathy pair, re-using one's own visceral and pain machinery to represent others' states.

• The medial prefrontal cortex, integrating traits, selves and others into standing person models and social norms.

• The premotor mirror-neuron territory, mapping observed action onto one's own motor vocabulary for goal reading.

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The superior temporal sulcus distributes its motion-and-gaze analysis dorsally to the temporoparietal junction and ventrally to the amygdala; the cingulum and uncinate connect medial prefrontal models with temporal and amygdalar stations; the arcuate's ventral neighbours link the system to language, through which most social evidence arrives after childhood; and this network is substantially the default mode network of the Part Four chapters, re-employed here for its social business.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

The fusiform station transforms pixels into person identity in about 170 milliseconds, the N170 potential.

The superior temporal sulcus transforms motion into intention-laden description: not limb trajectories but reaching, giving, evading - a coding so abstract it fires for animated triangles chasing one another.

The temporoparietal hand-off adds the decisive layer: the observed agent's informational history - what they saw and did not see - is used to construct their belief state as an object distinct from reality, the computation that passes the Sally-Anne false-belief test.

The medial prefrontal station adds durable person models and norms, and the insula-cingulate pair adds shared feeling, so the final representation is a predictive model of a person: identity, state, belief, goal, and what they will therefore do next.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

The face station runs the perceptual template motifs; the mirror territory runs an associative observation-execution motif; the temporoparietal station runs a model-holding attractor motif akin to Chapter 16's, keeping two world-models alive at once; the empathy pair runs interoceptive simulation motifs.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Face detection by 170 milliseconds; gaze following within 300 milliseconds; spontaneous belief tracking appears in anticipatory looking within about a second of the relevant event; explicit mentalising in conversation runs at the speech rate of Chapter 18.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

Stable relationship tracking is bounded near Dunbar's number, roughly 150; nested mentalising - he thinks that she knows that they intend - degrades sharply beyond four or five levels; explicit false-belief reasoning passes at about age four, though implicit versions appear earlier.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Oxytocin and vasopressin retune the whole system, modulating gaze to eyes, trust and in-group weighting; testosterone shifts it toward dominance reading; acute stress narrows empathy; and the amygdala gates how much any face matters at all.

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Person models update lifelong through a social prediction error - people surprising us - and cultural norms are installed wholesale in childhood through this system's coupling with language, the largest curriculum any system in this book receives.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Faces are preferred at birth, gaze following arrives by 9 to 12 months, joint attention and pointing by 12 to 18 months, explicit false belief by about 4 years, and the temporoparietal specialisation continues to sharpen through adolescence.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

Face and gaze machinery is old primate equipment; full false-belief attribution is at best marginal in great apes; the human elaboration co-evolved with language and with the longest childhood in nature, each requiring the others.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Oxytocin from the hypothalamus is this system's signature hormone, released in birth, contact and trust and returned as changed social weighting; cortisol reallocates it under threat; gonadal steroids shape its development and its adult biases.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Awake, the system runs whenever another agent is present or imagined, which for humans is nearly always; in slow-wave sleep its person models consolidate with the rest of declarative memory; in rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep it is conspicuously active, dreams being densely populated social simulations; under stress it contracts to threat reading.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Autism spectrum conditions involve altered development of this system, with explicit mentalising effortful rather than automatic; prosopagnosia removes the face station; psychopathy spares mentalising while blunting the insula-cingulate empathy pair, the inverse dissociation; social-anxiety disorder and paranoia are miscalibrations of its threat and intention priors; frontotemporal dementia can dissolve norms and empathy first.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

This system is why the organism lives in a world of persons rather than moving objects, and it is the biological seat of the framework's Layer 13 interface: every teacher, every institution, every reader of this manuscript is reached through it.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).
