◦ Faces, gaze and biological motion from the visual streams; prosody and speech content from the language system; own visceral state from interoception; oxytocin, cortisol and gonadal hormone levels.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Belief and intention attributions to executive and language systems; empathy states to valuation; trust and threat weightings to the reward economy; person-model updates to declarative memory.

• STATE.

◦ Active models of currently present agents; the standing gallery of roughly 150 tracked relationships; current perspective, self or other.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Face detection about 170 milliseconds; false-belief competence by about age 4; mentalising depth limit 4 to 5; relationship capacity roughly 150.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Face and gaze reading 170 to 300 milliseconds; belief updating within 1 second of new evidence; person models updated over years.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Autism-spectrum mentalising differences, prosopagnosia, psychopathy's empathy deficit, paranoid misattribution, frontotemporal norm dissolution.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

As a engineering pipeline this is the social_other_minds_modeller, subscribing to agent-perception and language topics and publishing per-agent belief, goal and feeling estimates as a keyed model store.

The nearest engineering analogue is a multi-agent state estimator, one recursive filter per tracked agent.

The biological difference is that each filter is built by partially simulating the self's own pipelines with another's inputs, so the modeller's accuracy about others is bounded by its access to itself.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to attribute a belief to an observed agent from what that agent has seen, and to predict the agent's action from the attributed belief rather than from reality.

Create a text variable called true location with initial value basket.

Create a table variable called agent belief with initial value empty.

When the agent observes an object placed at a location, record that location in the agent belief table.

When the object is moved while the agent is absent, perform the following actions.

    Update the true location to the new location.

    Leave the agent belief table unchanged, because the agent did not observe the move.

End the update step.

To predict where the agent will search, perform the following actions.

    Look up the location in the agent belief table.

    Predict a search at the believed location, not at the true location.

End the prediction step.

Check if the predicted search location differs from the true location.

If the check is true, then report that a false belief has been correctly attributed.

Display the true location, the believed location and the predicted search to the console.

The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because a belief is a dictionary keyed by agent, updated only on observation, and the false-belief test is a single comparison between that dictionary and the world.

# Belief attribution from observed action: the Sally-Anne
# false-belief computation of the right temporoparietal junction
# (Wimmer & Perner 1983; Saxe & Kanwisher, NeuroImage 2003).
world = {"marble": "basket"}
beliefs = {}                                  # agent -> their world model

def observe(agent, present=True):
    if present:
        beliefs.setdefault(agent, {}).update(world)   # sees reality

def move(obj, new_place, witnesses):
    world[obj] = new_place                    # reality changes
    for agent in witnesses:
        beliefs[agent][obj] = new_place       # only witnesses update

def predict_search(agent, obj):
    return beliefs[agent].get(obj)            # act on belief, not world

observe("sally"); observe("anne")             # both see marble in basket
# Sally leaves; Anne moves the marble to the box.
move("marble", "box", witnesses=["anne"])

for agent in ("sally", "anne"):
    guess = predict_search(agent, "marble")
    tag = "FALSE belief" if guess != world["marble"] else "true belief"
    print(f"{agent} will search the {guess}  ({tag})")
print("reality:", world["marble"])
# Passing the test requires predicting sally -> basket while
# knowing the marble is in the box: two models held at once.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The only system whose objects are other systems of its own kind: a network that watches hands and eyes and returns invisible entities - beliefs, intentions, feelings - well enough to predict what a person will do about a marble that is not where they think it is.


PART FOUR. THE LARGE-SCALE NETWORKS (CROSS-REFERENCED)

The ten resting-state networks were first catalogued at Layer 9, in sections 2.10.1 through 2.10.10 of the Layer 9 manuscript, because that is where their nodes are described.

A network of coupled regions is nevertheless a Layer 10 construct, and this part lifts each of the ten to its proper layer address.

The treatment here is deliberately condensed, per the corpus cross-reference convention: each aspect in one to three sentences, with the full node rosters, subscribe-and-publish contracts, and specification blocks left in their source sections.

The canonical seven-network parcellation of Yeo and colleagues (2011), from resting functional connectivity in one thousand subjects, underwrites the roster: visual, somatomotor, dorsal attention, ventral attention or salience, limbic, frontoparietal control, and default mode, here extended by the cingulo-opercular, language, and auditory networks.


CHAPTER 21. THE DEFAULT MODE NETWORK (The task-negative midline network) (The Inward Storyteller) (COG)

Close your eyes in the scanner, do nothing, and one constellation of midline and lateral regions brightens while the task networks dim: the brain's default is not silence but self-generated thought.

Inward names its direction of gaze, toward memory, self, and imagined futures rather than the senses.

Storyteller names its product, a continuous stream of recombined autobiographical and social material.

SOURCE: This chapter is a cross-reference. The full catalogue entry is section 2.10.1 of the Layer 9 manuscript, LAYER_09_NATURES_COGNITIVE_ARCHITECTURE_MANUSCRIPT.txt.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The Default Mode Network (DMN) is the inward-facing network, most active when attention leaves the outside world, serving autobiographical and prospective memory, self-reference, mentalising about others, moral reasoning, and mind-wandering (section 2.10.1).

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

Medial prefrontal cortex, posterior cingulate cortex and precuneus, the bilateral angular gyri, lateral temporal cortex, and the hippocampal formation; the full roster with jobs is in section 2.10.1.

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The cingulum bundle links the medial prefrontal and posterior cingulate hubs, and the hippocampus feeds the network through the parahippocampal and retrosplenial route (section 2.10.1, INPUTS).

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Mnemonic fragments from the hippocampal formation are recombined at the midline hubs into scenes, selves, and simulations, then broadcast; the hand-offs add self-relevance and value at the medial prefrontal stage.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

Association-cortex recurrent-attractor motifs of the microcircuits catalogue sustain its internally-generated states.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Slow, sustained activation over seconds, anticorrelated with the task-positive networks; a mind-wandering episode spans roughly 5 to 30 seconds.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

One narrative stream at a time; engagement collapses within about a second when external demand rises.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Dopamine, acetylcholine, and the arousal systems set its engagement, and the salience network holds its off-switch (section 2.10.1, MODULATION).

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Its coupling strengthens with experience and is disrupted in ageing and disease.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Its long-range midline coupling matures late, across childhood into the third decade.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

A homologue exists in macaques and even rodents, but the human network is enlarged with the association cortices it inhabits.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Cortisol shifts activity away from this network toward salience processing under stress.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Dominant in quiet waking rest, altered in rapid-eye-movement (REM) dreaming, fragmented in deep slow-wave sleep, and suppressed under acute stress.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Hyperconnectivity with rumination in depression; early amyloid deposition and decoupling in Alzheimer disease.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

It is why an idle human is never mentally idle: remembering, planning, and rehearsing conversations fill every unclaimed moment.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

Condensed; the full subscribe-and-publish contract with values is the specification block of section 2.10.1 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Self-generated thought when external demand is low.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Recombined memory, simulation, and self-relevant content.

• STATE.

◦ Engagement level anticorrelated with task networks.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Core triad coupling strengths.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Seconds-scale episodes.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Rumination lock-in in depression.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

default_mode_network is a broadcast module that publishes internally-generated content whenever the task-positive topics fall quiet, nearest analogue an idle-loop background job, differing in that its output is constructive simulation, not housekeeping.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to generate inward content when external demand is low.
Create a number variable called external demand with initial value 0.2.
Create a number variable called engagement with initial value 0.
Create a list variable called memory store with initial value of five memory fragments.
Set engagement to the difference of 1 and external demand.
If engagement is greater than 0.5 is true, then perform the following actions.
    Select two random fragments from memory store.
    Combine the two fragments into one imagined scene.
    Publish the imagined scene to the broadcast topic.
End the conditional block.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because random recombination of a memory list under a demand gate is three transparent lines.

# DMN signature: self-generated recombination when external demand is low.
# (Raichle et al., PNAS 2001; Yeo et al., J Neurophysiol 2011, section 2.10.1.)
import random
memories = ["the beach trip", "tomorrow's meeting", "an old friend",
            "the unpaid bill", "a childhood kitchen"]
for demand in (0.1, 0.4, 0.9):
    engagement = 1.0 - demand
    if engagement > 0.5:
        scene = " meets ".join(random.sample(memories, 2))
        print(f"demand {demand}: DMN imagines: {scene}")
    else:
        print(f"demand {demand}: DMN suppressed by task-positive networks")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The only network that switches on when the task switches off.


CHAPTER 22. THE SALIENCE / VENTRAL ATTENTION NETWORK (The insular-cingulate detection network) (The Alarm Switchboard) (COG)

Something moves at the edge of vision, or a heartbeat stumbles, and within a few hundred milliseconds the whole brain changes mode: this small network decides what suddenly matters.

Alarm names its trigger, the salient and the unexpected.

Switchboard names its power, toggling control between the inward default mode and the outward executive networks.

SOURCE: This chapter is a cross-reference. The full catalogue entry is section 2.10.2 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The Salience Network (SN), with its Ventral Attention Network (VAN) component, detects the most behaviourally relevant event and reorients attention to it, switching the brain between inward and outward modes (section 2.10.2).

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

The bilateral anterior insula (right-dominant), the dorsal anterior cingulate cortex, and for the ventral attention component the temporoparietal junction and ventral inferior frontal cortex (section 2.10.2).

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

Insula and cingulate are joined by a direct fronto-insular tract carrying the salience estimate, and outputs run to brainstem autonomic centres.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Interoceptive, sensory, and threat inputs are reduced at the insula to a single salience estimate, which the cingulate converts into a switching command.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

Comparator motifs, plus the large fast von Economo neuron projections special to these two regions.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Phasic: a detection-and-switch event completes in roughly 100 to 500 milliseconds.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

One winner at a time; it flags the single most salient event and discards the rest.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Norepinephrine carries surprise, with dopamine and acetylcholine shaping what counts as salient (section 2.10.2, MODULATION).

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

It learns what is salient by experience.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Insular-cingulate coupling strengthens through adolescence as control matures.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

Von Economo neurons in the same two hubs appear only in large-brained social species.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

It triggers, and is sensitised by, the cortisol stress axis, and it publishes autonomic set-points downward.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Vigilant while awake, damped in slow-wave sleep, and hyperactive under stress, when it locks the brain into the outward mode.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Aberrant salience assignment in psychosis; insular hyperreactivity in anxiety; early degeneration in behavioural-variant frontotemporal dementia.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

It is why a name spoken across a noisy room, or a missed step on a stair, seizes the whole mind at once.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

Condensed; the full subscribe-and-publish contract with values is the specification block of section 2.10.2 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Interoceptive salience and unexpected events.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ The switch command between default and executive configurations.

• STATE.

◦ Which coalition currently holds the workspace.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Salience thresholds.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Hundreds of milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Misassigned salience in psychosis and anxiety.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

salience_switchboard subscribes to every sensory and interoceptive topic and publishes a single network-switch command, nearest analogue an interrupt controller, differing in that its priorities are learned and hormone-tuned.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to switch control between the default mode and executive networks.
Create a number variable called salience with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called switch threshold with initial value 0.6.
Create a text variable called active network with initial value "default mode".
Set salience to the largest of the interoceptive, sensory, and threat signals.
If salience is greater than switch threshold is true, then perform the following actions.
    Set active network to "executive".
    Publish an interrupt to the default mode network.
Otherwise, perform the following actions.
    Set active network to "default mode".
End the conditional block.
Display active network to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because a max-and-threshold interrupt is clearest as plain conditionals.

# Salience network signature: the anterior insula toggles control between
# the default mode and executive networks (Sridharan, Levitin and Menon,
# PNAS 2008; section 2.10.2).
THRESHOLD = 0.6
events = [("heartbeat steady", 0.1), ("phone buzz", 0.5),
          ("own name heard", 0.9), ("quiet room", 0.2)]
active = "default_mode"
for name, salience in events:
    if salience > THRESHOLD and active == "default_mode":
        active = "executive"
    elif salience <= THRESHOLD:
        active = "default_mode"
    print(f"{name:16s} salience {salience:.1f} -> {active}")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The right anterior insula shows greater causal outflow than any node of the two big networks it commands.


CHAPTER 23. THE DORSAL ATTENTION NETWORK (The frontoparietal orienting network) (The Deliberate Spotlight) (COG)

Decide to watch the left doorway and, before anything appears there, neurons representing that patch of space raise their gain: attention aimed on purpose.

Deliberate names its top-down, goal-driven character, in contrast to the reflexive ventral system of chapter 22.

Spotlight names its output, a beam of gain moved across the sensory field.

SOURCE: This chapter is a cross-reference. The full catalogue entry is section 2.10.3 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The Dorsal Attention Network (DAN) aims visuospatial attention voluntarily at a chosen place or feature and selects targets for the eyes and hands (section 2.10.3).

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

The frontal eye fields bilaterally, the intraparietal sulcus and superior parietal lobule, and the motion area MT-plus (section 2.10.3).

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The superior longitudinal fasciculus joins frontal eye fields to parietal cortex, and gaze commands leave for the superior colliculus.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Goals from the executive network become a parietal spatial priority map, which becomes multiplicative gain applied to visual cortex and a gaze command.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

Winner-take-all priority-map motifs and gain-modulation motifs of the microcircuits catalogue.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

A voluntary attention shift takes roughly 200 to 300 milliseconds, and covert attention can move independently of the eyes.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

About four tracked objects, and attentional gain raises firing on the order of tens of percent, not tenfold.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Acetylcholine supplies attentional gain, with norepinephrine and dopamine secondary (section 2.10.3, MODULATION).

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Attentional templates are learned; experts search their domain faster.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Sustained voluntary attention lengthens across childhood with parietal-frontal maturation.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

Homologous frontal-eye-field and parietal machinery is well documented in the macaque.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Thyroid hormone sets its alertness floor, and cortisol narrows its beam under stress.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

On duty only while awake; offline in sleep; stress biases control away from it toward stimulus-driven capture.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Right-hemisphere damage to its parietal node yields hemineglect; its dysregulation figures in attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

It is why the searched-for key leaps out of the cluttered drawer.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

Condensed; the full subscribe-and-publish contract with values is the specification block of section 2.10.3 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Goal-driven spatial priorities.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Top-down attention biases to sensory cortex.

• STATE.

◦ Current attentional locus.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Bias gains.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Hundreds of milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Hemispatial neglect after right-lateralised damage.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

dorsal_attention_spotlight subscribes to goal topics and publishes gain fields to sensory modules and gaze commands, nearest analogue a scheduler assigning priority boosts, differing in that its resource is neural gain, not processor time.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to apply top-down gain at a chosen location.
Create a number variable called attended location with initial value 2.
Create a number variable called gain with initial value 1.5.
Create a list variable called scene with one signal strength per location.
For each location in the scene, perform the following actions.
    If the location equals attended location is true, then perform the following actions.
        Multiply the signal at the location by gain.
    End the conditional block.
End the loop.
Select the location with the largest signal as the target.
Publish a gaze command toward the target.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because a priority map with multiplicative gain is one list comprehension.

# DAN signature: a spatial priority map applies multiplicative gain
# to the attended location (Corbetta and Shulman, Nat Rev Neurosci 2002;
# section 2.10.3).
scene = [0.5, 0.4, 0.45, 0.6]          # bottom-up signal at four locations
GAIN = 1.5                             # cholinergic attentional gain
for attended in (None, 2):
    boosted = [s * (GAIN if i == attended else 1.0)
               for i, s in enumerate(scene)]
    target = boosted.index(max(boosted))
    print(f"attend {attended}: map {boosted} -> gaze to location {target}")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The only network whose output is a moveable beam of gain aimed by intention rather than by the stimulus.


CHAPTER 24. THE FRONTOPARIETAL CONTROL NETWORK (The central executive network) (The Flexible Governor) (COG)

Hold a telephone number in mind while searching for a pen and two things persist against every distraction: the digits and the goal, both kept alive by this network.

Flexible names its defining trait, reconfiguring itself and every other network per the task of the moment.

Governor names its role, the controller that holds goals and issues biases.

SOURCE: This chapter is a cross-reference. The full catalogue entry is section 2.10.4 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The Frontoparietal Control Network (FPN), also called the Central Executive Network (CEN), serves working memory, cognitive control, goal maintenance, task-switching, and decision-making, the core of fluid intelligence (section 2.10.4).

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

Dorsolateral and frontopolar prefrontal cortex, anterior inferior parietal lobule, dorsal precuneus, midcingulate cortex, the head of the caudate, and the mediodorsal thalamus (section 2.10.4).

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The superior longitudinal fasciculus binds prefrontal and parietal nodes, and the mediodorsal thalamus and caudate close a gating loop under the cortex.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Sensory evidence and value signals are integrated into a maintained goal state, which is transformed into control biases addressed to attention, sensory, and motor systems, plus a suppression signal to the default mode network.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

Persistent-activity attractor motifs for working memory and thalamo-striatal gating motifs from the microcircuits catalogue.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Persistent delay-period activity over seconds to tens of seconds, with task-switch costs of roughly 100 to 300 milliseconds.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

Working memory holds about four chunks (range three to five), the sharpest capacity limit in cognition.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Dopamine sets prefrontal gain on an inverted-U, with norepinephrine and acetylcholine assisting, and the salience network supplies its engagement trigger (section 2.10.4, MODULATION).

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Training strengthens its control; it is the substrate of skill in reasoning.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The last network to mature, its prefrontal nodes myelinating into the mid-twenties.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

Its lateral prefrontal territory is among the most expanded cortex in humans relative to other primates.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Acute cortisol impairs its working memory; thyroid and gonadal hormones tune its efficiency.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Fully engaged only in alert waking; largely offline in slow-wave and rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep, which is one reason dreams go unexamined; degraded under stress as control shifts to habit systems.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Its dysfunction is central to schizophrenia's cognitive symptoms, to attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder, and to executive deficits after frontal injury.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

It is the difference between reacting to the world and running a plan through it.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

Condensed; the full subscribe-and-publish contract with values is the specification block of section 2.10.4 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Goals, rules, and feedback.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Flexible task-set control signals.

• STATE.

◦ The active task set.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Rule and gain settings.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Seconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Executive dysfunction after prefrontal injury.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

frontoparietal_governor subscribes to salience, evidence, and value topics and publishes control biases plus a default-mode suppression signal, nearest analogue a supervisory control loop, differing in that the controller itself is retuned by dopamine on an inverted-U.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to maintain a goal and bias other systems by it.
Create a text variable called goal with initial value "find the pen".
Create a list variable called working memory with capacity 4.
Create a number variable called distraction with initial value 0.
While the goal is not achieved, perform the following actions.
    Refresh every item held in working memory.
    Publish a control bias favouring goal-relevant signals.
    Publish a suppression signal to the default mode network.
    If distraction is greater than 0.7 is true, then perform the following actions.
        Restore the goal from working memory.
    End the conditional block.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because a capacity-limited store with goal-driven biasing fits in a dozen lines.

# FPN signature: hold a goal in a 4-slot store and bias processing by it
# (Cowan, Behav Brain Sci 2001; Duncan, Trends Cogn Sci 2010; section 2.10.4).
CAPACITY = 4
working_memory = []
for item in ["goal:find pen", "digit 7", "digit 4", "digit 9", "digit 2"]:
    if len(working_memory) >= CAPACITY:
        dropped = working_memory.pop(1)          # goal slot is protected
        print(f"capacity hit: dropped {dropped}")
    working_memory.append(item)
goal = working_memory[0]
signals = {"pen": 0.4, "phone": 0.6}
bias = {k: v + (0.3 if k in goal else 0.0) for k, v in signals.items()}
print(f"store {working_memory}; biased signals {bias}")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The one network whose job is to reconfigure all the others.


CHAPTER 25. THE CINGULO-OPERCULAR NETWORK (The task-set maintenance network) (The Steady Helmsman) (COG)

An hour into a tedious vigilance task, something is still holding the instructions in force between one decision and the next: not the flexible governor of chapter 24, but its steadier partner.

Steady names its tonic, across-trials time-scale.

Helmsman names its job, holding the set course and logging every error against it.

SOURCE: This chapter is a cross-reference. The full catalogue entry is section 2.10.5 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The Cingulo-Opercular Network (CON) provides tonic alertness, stable task-set maintenance, and error and feedback monitoring, sustaining control across trials while the frontoparietal network adjusts within them (section 2.10.5).

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

The dorsal anterior cingulate and adjacent medial superior frontal cortex, the anterior insula and frontal operculum, anterior prefrontal cortex, and the thalamus (section 2.10.5).

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

Cingulate and opercular nodes couple through medial frontal tracts, with thalamic loops carrying arousal from the ascending systems.

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Task instructions become a maintained set-point; performance feedback is compared against it and emitted as error signals that adjust control.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

Slow integrator motifs for set maintenance and comparator motifs for error monitoring.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Tonic activation across whole task blocks of minutes, punctuated by phasic error responses near 100 milliseconds after a mistake (the error-related negativity).

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

One task-set held at a time; vigilance measurably decays over roughly 30 minutes without reinforcement.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Norepinephrine sets its tonic arousal, with acetylcholine and dopamine secondary (section 2.10.5, MODULATION).

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

It learns the value of maintaining versus switching sets.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Its stable set-maintenance signal emerges through late childhood, later than reactive control.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

It shares the insular-cingulate core, and the von Economo neurons, of the salience network from which analyses only lately distinguished it.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Cortisol and thyroid state set the tonic alertness it maintains.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

It is the waking state's keel; its collapse marks sleep onset, and under stress its error signals amplify.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Implicated in the disorganisation of schizophrenia, in obsessive-compulsive disorder's overactive error signal, and in vigilance failure after sleep deprivation.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

It is why the proofreader is still applying the rules on page two hundred.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

Condensed; the full subscribe-and-publish contract with values is the specification block of section 2.10.5 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Task onset and error signals.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Sustained task-mode maintenance and alerting.

• STATE.

◦ Tonic task engagement.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Stability parameters.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Minutes-scale sustained activity.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Performance lapses when tonic control fails.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

cinguloopercular_helmsman subscribes to instruction, feedback, and arousal topics and publishes a tonic set signal plus error corrections, nearest analogue a watchdog service with a persisted configuration, differing in that its configuration is a learned, hormone-tuned neural state.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to hold a task-set across trials and correct by error.
Create a text variable called task set with initial value "respond to red only".
Create a number variable called set strength with initial value 1.0.
Create a number variable called decay with initial value 0.02.
For each trial, perform the following actions.
    Publish the task set at the current set strength.
    Subtract decay from set strength.
    If the trial outcome is an error is true, then perform the following actions.
        Add 0.2 to set strength.
        Publish an error signal to the executive network.
    End the conditional block.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because tonic decay plus error-driven boosting is a five-line loop.

# CON signature: tonic task-set maintenance with error-driven correction
# (Dosenbach et al., Neuron 2006; section 2.10.5).
strength, DECAY, BOOST = 1.0, 0.02, 0.2
outcomes = ["ok", "ok", "error", "ok", "ok", "error", "ok"]
for trial, outcome in enumerate(outcomes, 1):
    strength -= DECAY                       # vigilance decays every trial
    if outcome == "error":
        strength += BOOST                   # error signal re-arms the set
    print(f"trial {trial}: {outcome:5s} set-strength {strength:.2f}")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The slow, steady counterpart to the fast frontoparietal controller: minutes, not moments.


CHAPTER 26. THE LANGUAGE NETWORK (The perisylvian language network) (The Meaning Loom) (COG)

A pressure wave strikes the ear and half a second later it is a proposition about the world; a thought forms and half a second later the tongue is executing it: no other species runs this loop.

Meaning names the currency at the loom's centre.

Loom names the weaving of sound, syntax, and sense into one fabric, and back again.

SOURCE: This chapter is a cross-reference. The full catalogue entry is section 2.10.6 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The language network turns sound and text into meaning and meaning back into speech, serving comprehension, production, syntax, semantics, and phonology (section 2.10.6).

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

Broca's area (inferior frontal gyrus, areas 44 and 45), Wernicke's area (posterior superior temporal gyrus and sulcus), the middle temporal gyrus, and the angular and supramarginal gyri (section 2.10.6).

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The arcuate fasciculus carries the dorsal sound-to-articulation route, the extreme capsule the ventral sound-to-meaning route, and the frontal aslant tract links to the pre-supplementary motor area (section 2.10.6).

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Spectrotemporal patterns become phonemes, phonemes words, words parsed syntactic structures bearing meaning, and in production the same fabric is unwoven into articulatory programs.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

Sequence-generation and prediction motifs of the microcircuits catalogue, chained into a parser.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Comprehension is incremental at roughly 3 to 6 syllables per second, with semantic anomaly detected near 400 milliseconds and syntactic revision near 600.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

Centre-embedded clauses overwhelm it at about two levels, and production runs near 2 to 3 words per second.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Dopamine and acetylcholine modulate it, and left-hemisphere lateralisation is its structural gate in most people (section 2.10.6, MODULATION).

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Language is learned, with the strongest plasticity in the critical period of early childhood.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The arcuate fasciculus myelinates across childhood as vocabulary and syntax grow.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

Its tracts exist in apes in reduced form; the massively expanded human arcuate fasciculus is a signature difference.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Thyroid hormone is required for its normal development; gonadal hormones contribute to its lateralisation.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Fluent while awake, generator of inner speech at rest, loosely active in rapid-eye-movement (REM) dream dialogue, silent in deep sleep.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Broca (non-fluent) and Wernicke (fluent) aphasia from damage to its poles; conduction aphasia from arcuate damage; developmental dyslexia and stuttering from its miswiring.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

It gives the organism a second world, the world of things said, in which most of human life is conducted.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

Condensed; the full subscribe-and-publish contract with values is the specification block of section 2.10.6 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Phonological, lexical, and syntactic streams.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Comprehension and production coordination.

• STATE.

◦ Current linguistic frame.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Dual-stream couplings.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Hundreds of milliseconds per word.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ The aphasias.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

language_meaning_loom subscribes to auditory and visual word topics and publishes semantic content and articulatory programs, nearest analogue a bidirectional compiler between a serial token stream and a structured representation, differing in that both grammars are learned and probabilistic.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to map a word sequence to meaning incrementally.
Create a list variable called word stream with an incoming sentence.
Create a structure variable called parse with initial value empty.
For each word in the word stream, perform the following actions.
    Look up the meaning of the word.
    Attach the meaning to the parse at the predicted position.
    If the attachment fails is true, then perform the following actions.
        Revise the parse and reattach the meaning.
    End the conditional block.
End the loop.
Publish the completed parse as semantic content.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because incremental parsing with prediction and revision reads naturally as a loop over tokens.

# Language network signature: incremental sound-to-meaning mapping with
# prediction, revision costs near 400-600 ms (Hickok and Poeppel,
# Nat Rev Neurosci 2007; section 2.10.6).
lexicon = {"the": "DET", "dog": "NOUN", "bit": "VERB", "man": "NOUN"}
expected, parse = "DET", []
for word in "the dog bit the man".split():
    tag = lexicon[word]
    cost = 0 if tag == expected else 600      # revision cost in milliseconds
    parse.append((word, tag))
    expected = {"DET": "NOUN", "NOUN": "VERB", "VERB": "DET"}[tag]
    print(f"{word:4s} -> {tag:4s} revision-cost {cost} ms")
print("published meaning:", parse)

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The only network with a wholly species-typical faculty and a left-hemisphere monopoly to run it.


CHAPTER 27. THE SENSORIMOTOR NETWORK (The perception-action loop network) (The Body Loop) (NON-COG)

Every reach, step, and syllable of speech runs through one closed loop: sense the body, move it, sense the result.

Body names its territory, the map of muscle and skin along the central sulcus.

Loop names its logic, command out and feedback in, closed in tens of milliseconds.

SOURCE: This chapter is a cross-reference. The full catalogue entry is section 2.10.7 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The Sensorimotor Network (SMN) executes movement and processes somatosensory feedback, the closed loop of embodied action (section 2.10.7).

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

Primary motor cortex, primary somatosensory cortex, the supplementary motor area, and premotor cortex, often divided into hand, foot, and mouth sub-networks (section 2.10.7).

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

Commands leave by the corticospinal tract, feedback returns by the ventral posterior thalamus, and efference copies go to the cerebellum and parietal cortex (section 2.10.7).

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Plans from premotor and supplementary areas become muscle-level commands in primary motor cortex and are corrected against returning proprioceptive and tactile feedback.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

Canonical layered cortical relay motifs plus the spinal pattern-generator interfaces they command.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

The fastest cortical loop: transcortical feedback corrections in 50 to 100 milliseconds, movement-related beta-band rhythms near 20 hertz.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

Corticospinal conduction runs up to roughly 70 metres per second, and skilled sequences saturate near 10 movements per second.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Dopamine supplies vigour, with acetylcholine and norepinephrine tuning excitability, and the basal ganglia gate which command is released (section 2.10.7, MODULATION).

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Motor learning reshapes it continuously; its maps enlarge with practice.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

The first cortical network to show adult-like resting coupling, functional before birth.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

The most conserved of the ten, with direct cortico-motoneuronal connections for the hand a primate refinement.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Thyroid hormone sets muscle-command tempo, and adrenaline raises loop gain in emergency.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Active in waking action, running sleep spindles over it in stage two sleep for consolidation, and actively paralysed at its spinal exit during rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Hemiplegia after stroke in its cortex or tract; Parkinson disease starves it of vigour; amyotrophic lateral sclerosis kills its output neurons.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

Every intention the other nine networks form becomes real only through this one.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

Condensed; the full subscribe-and-publish contract with values is the specification block of section 2.10.7 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Body-map sensory and motor traffic.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Integrated sensorimotor readiness.

• STATE.

◦ Current body configuration.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Somatotopic couplings.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Tens of milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Deafferentation and motor network disorders.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

sensorimotor_body_loop subscribes to plan and feedback topics and publishes motor commands with efference copies, nearest analogue a closed-loop motion controller, differing in that its plant model is learned and its gains are neuromodulated.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The only network whose output moves matter.


CHAPTER 28. THE VISUAL NETWORK (The occipital hierarchy network) (The Light Refinery) (NON-COG)

Two small images of points of light enter at the back of the skull and leave, a few synapses later, as objects, faces, places, and motion.

Light names its raw material.

Refinery names its method, staged transformation of a crude input into progressively purer products.

SOURCE: This chapter is a cross-reference. The full catalogue entry is section 2.10.8 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The visual network performs visual perception across the whole hierarchy, from edges to objects, scenes, and motion (section 2.10.8).

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

Primary visual cortex, the extrastriate areas through the fourth visual area, the motion complex MT-plus, and the ventral (object) and dorsal (spatial) stream extensions (section 2.10.8).

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The retinal image arrives by way of the lateral geniculate nucleus, attentional selection by way of the pulvinar, and two output streams leave for parietal and temporal cortex (section 2.10.8).

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Points of light become oriented edges in primary visual cortex, contours and colours in the intermediate areas, and objects, faces, and scenes in the ventral stream, with receptive fields growing from fractions of a degree to tens of degrees.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

The canonical six-layer feedforward-plus-feedback cortical motif, repeated at every station with wider pooling.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

A feedforward sweep reaches object recognition in roughly 150 milliseconds, with recurrent refinement continuing for hundreds more.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

About one megabyte per second of optic-nerve input is compressed to a scene gist graspable in a single 100 millisecond glance.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Acetylcholine and norepinephrine set its gain, and the dorsal attention network applies top-down selection (section 2.10.8, MODULATION).

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Perceptual learning tunes it within a strong developmental critical period.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Its maps self-organise from retinal activity before birth and are refined by seen experience in the critical period.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

The primate lineage's speciality: about a third of human cortex serves vision.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Minimal beyond general thyroid and metabolic dependence; it is the least hormone-coupled of the ten.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Faithful while awake, replaying in slow-wave consolidation, and internally driven during rapid-eye-movement (REM) dreams, when it renders imagery with no retinal input.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Cortical blindness from primary visual cortex damage; prosopagnosia and visual agnosia from ventral stream lesions; motion blindness from MT-plus loss.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

It makes the human a predominantly visual animal, trusting the eye over every other sense.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

Condensed; the full subscribe-and-publish contract with values is the specification block of section 2.10.8 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Retinotopic visual traffic.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Integrated visual field processing.

• STATE.

◦ Current visual configuration.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Retinotopic couplings.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Tens of milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Cortical visual impairment.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

visual_light_refinery subscribes to the geniculate image topic and publishes object and spatial streams, nearest analogue a staged processing pipeline, differing in that every stage also receives feedback from the stages above it.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The clearest hierarchy in the brain: every station one step more abstract than the last.


CHAPTER 29. THE AUDITORY NETWORK (The temporal hearing network) (The Sound Sifter) (NON-COG)

Air vibrates, the cochlea unrolls the vibration into frequencies, and this network sifts the result into voices, footsteps, and music.

Sound names its raw material, delivered already frequency-sorted.

Sifter names its work, separating overlapping sources and patterns in time.

SOURCE: This chapter is a cross-reference. The full catalogue entry is section 2.10.9 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The auditory network performs auditory perception, sound-pattern and speech-sound analysis, and the precursors of language and music (section 2.10.9).

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

Primary auditory cortex on Heschl's gyrus, the auditory belt and parabelt, and the superior temporal gyrus and sulcus (section 2.10.9).

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

Sound arrives by way of the medial geniculate nucleus, and spectrotemporal patterns leave for the language network, the multisensory superior temporal sulcus, and prefrontal cortex (section 2.10.9).

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

A tonotopic frequency map becomes belt-level spectrotemporal patterns, then parabelt-level categories such as phonemes and melodic contours.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

The canonical cortical motif tuned for time: faster synapses and finer temporal following than its visual counterpart.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

The brain's fastest sensory timing, resolving gaps of 2 to 3 milliseconds and interaural delays of tens of microseconds.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

Covers roughly 20 hertz to 20 kilohertz across about 120 decibels of dynamic range, but follows only one attended stream well at a time.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Acetylcholine, norepinephrine, and attention set its gain, sharpening the attended stream at a cocktail party (section 2.10.9, MODULATION).

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Auditory and speech learning tune it, enlarging representations of trained sounds.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Hearing begins in the womb; native-language phoneme tuning is measurable within the first year.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

A conserved mammalian plan whose human parabelt is specialised toward speech.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

Minimal beyond thyroid dependence in development; congenital hypothyroidism impairs hearing.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

The last sense to fade at sleep onset and the sentry of sleep itself, still evaluating salient sounds, which is why a name or an infant's cry can wake the sleeper.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Cortical deafness and pure word deafness from bilateral lesions; tinnitus as its deafferented map's phantom; presbycusis narrowing its range with age.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

It keeps the organism connected to events behind it, around corners, and in the dark.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

Condensed; the full subscribe-and-publish contract with values is the specification block of section 2.10.9 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Tonotopic auditory traffic.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Integrated auditory scene processing.

• STATE.

◦ Current auditory configuration.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Tonotopic couplings.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Milliseconds to tens of milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Central auditory processing disorders.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

auditory_sound_sifter subscribes to the geniculate spectrotemporal topic and publishes pattern streams to language and multisensory modules, nearest analogue a streaming digital signal processor, differing in that its filters are learned and attention-gated.

This section does not apply to this system for the reason that this system serves an effector, relay, regulatory, or economic role rather than a cognitive-computational role, and is tagged (NON-COG).

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The only network that never sleeps on duty.


CHAPTER 30. THE LIMBIC NETWORK (The orbitofrontal-temporopolar valuation network) (The Worth Weigher) (COG)

Nothing in the sensory stream carries a price tag; every value the organism acts on is computed here, where feeling, memory, and body-state meet.

Worth names its currency, the common scale on which apples, insults, and futures are compared.

Weigher names its operation, integrating evidence, memory, and gut-state into one appraisal.

SOURCE: This chapter is a cross-reference. The full catalogue entry is section 2.10.10 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The limbic network computes valuation and emotion and integrates affect with memory and decision, joining reward to cognition (section 2.10.10).

THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

The orbitofrontal cortex, ventromedial prefrontal cortex, and temporal pole, coupled with the subcortical amygdala, hippocampus, and ventral striatum (section 2.10.10).

THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The uncinate fasciculus joins orbitofrontal cortex to the temporal pole and amygdala, and outputs run to the hypothalamus, brainstem autonomic centres, and striatum (section 2.10.10, OUTPUTS).

THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

Sensory and mnemonic content, amygdala emotional tags, and interoceptive state are integrated into a single value-and-affect appraisal published to cognition and to the body.

THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

Integrator-and-appraiser motifs, with amygdala associative-learning microcircuits and ventral striatal reward gates.

TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Value signals emerge within 150 to 300 milliseconds of a stimulus, while moods set by the same network persist for hours.

CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

Values are computed relative to context and reference points, not absolutely, which makes the weigher fast but systematically biasable.

MODULATION AND GATING:

Dopamine, serotonin, norepinephrine, the neuropeptides, and cortisol all retune it, the broadest modulator list of the ten (section 2.10.10, MODULATION).

PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Reward and emotional learning reshape it continuously, gated by dopamine.

DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Its subcortical partners mature early and its orbitofrontal regulator late, an imbalance that shapes adolescent risk-taking.

EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

The most ancient cognitive network, built on olfactory and hippocampal cortex older than the six-layered sheet.

THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

The strongest of the ten: it drives the hypothalamic axes downward, and cortisol, gonadal, and metabolic hormones feed back on its every node.

STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Active in waking valuation, replayed with the hippocampus in slow-wave sleep, dominant in the emotional narratives of rapid-eye-movement (REM) sleep, and biased toward threat under stress.

DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Depression and anxiety as disorders of its appraisals; addiction as capture of its reward machinery; Kluver-Bucy syndrome from bilateral temporal damage.

HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

It decides what everything is worth, and thereby what the organism will do next and how the doing will feel.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

Condensed; the full subscribe-and-publish contract with values is the specification block of section 2.10.10 of the Layer 9 manuscript.

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Value, emotion, and memory traffic.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ Affective colouring of ongoing cognition.

• STATE.

◦ Current affective tone.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Limbic couplings.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Seconds and slower.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Mood disorder network signatures.

THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

limbic_worth_weigher subscribes to sensory, mnemonic, and interoceptive topics and publishes value and affect signals to cognition and drive to the body, nearest analogue a learned utility function inside a control loop, differing in that its outputs are also felt.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to integrate evidence, memory, and body-state into one value.
Create a number variable called sensory value with the current stimulus estimate.
Create a number variable called memory value with the remembered outcome for this stimulus.
Create a number variable called body state with the current interoceptive signal.
Create a number variable called appraisal with initial value 0.
Set appraisal to the weighted sum of sensory value, memory value, and body state.
Publish appraisal to the executive and default mode networks.
If appraisal is less than 0 is true, then perform the following actions.
    Publish an avoidance drive to the hypothalamus and striatum.
Otherwise, perform the following actions.
    Publish an approach drive to the hypothalamus and striatum.
End the conditional block.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because a weighted appraisal with an approach-or-avoid readout is a single expression per option.

# Limbic network signature: integrate sense, memory, and body-state into
# one value and publish approach or avoidance (section 2.10.10).
W_SENSE, W_MEMORY, W_BODY = 0.4, 0.4, 0.2
options = {"ripe fruit":   (0.8,  0.9,  0.3),
           "spoiled food": (0.2, -0.8, -0.5),
           "rival's glare": (-0.4, -0.6, -0.7)}
for name, (sense, memory, body) in options.items():
    value = W_SENSE * sense + W_MEMORY * memory + W_BODY * body
    action = "approach" if value >= 0 else "avoid"
    print(f"{name:14s} value {value:+.2f} -> {action}")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The only network whose computations are experienced as feelings.


PART FIVE. THE NEUROMODULATORY BROADCAST SYSTEMS

Each of the six systems in this part is treated end-to-end as one organ: a small huddle of source neurons deep in the brainstem or hypothalamus, a projection map that fans out over the whole forebrain, a field of receptor territories that read the broadcast differently in each place, and a computational role that tunes the machine rather than joining the message.

These are the mode-setting systems every modes volume points to: they do not carry the picture, the word, or the plan, but they set the gain, the patience, the wakefulness, and the learning rate with which every other system does its work.

Where the sensory and motor chains of Parts One and Two are point-to-point wiring, these systems are the framework's volume transmission channel considered as a system, spraying a slow chemical mist into the extracellular space so that one thousand-cell nucleus can retune a billion synapses at once.

Four of them carry the (COG) tag because they teach, gate, and reweight cognition itself, and two carry the (NON-COG) tag because they supply the wakeful substrate on which cognition runs; together they are the reason the same cortex behaves like a different organ at dawn, at noon, in terror, and in sleep.


CHAPTER 31. THE DOPAMINE SYSTEM (The Mesostriatal and Mesocorticolimbic Dopaminergic Projection) (The Reward-Teaching Broadcast) (COG)

Somewhere behind your eyes, ten thousand pigmented cells the size of a lentil are deciding whether the world just turned out better or worse than you expected, and stamping that verdict onto every habit you own.

This is the smallest teacher in the brain and the one that prices the whole economy of choice.

Reward names what it broadcasts: not pleasure itself but the surprise in pleasure, the gap between what arrived and what was predicted.

Teaching names its office: it does not decide, it trains the deciders, writing its error signal into the weights of the selection loops.

Broadcast names its reach: a few tens of thousands of cells whose axons touch nearly every neuron of the striatum and much of the frontal cortex at once.


WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The dopamine system is the set of midbrain neurons that make dopamine, a small signalling molecule of the catecholamine family, and spray it across the striatum and cortex to teach the brain which actions and cues predict reward.

Its headline job is to compute and broadcast the reward prediction error, the difference between the reward that arrived and the reward that was expected, and to use that error as a teaching signal.

Its second job is to set vigour, the speed and force with which the organism is willing to move, so that a brain expecting rich rewards acts briskly and a brain expecting little moves slowly.

The system therefore does two things at two speeds: fast bursts that teach, and a slow background level that motivates.


THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

• The ventral tegmental area, the medial midbrain cell group whose dopamine neurons project to the ventral striatum and frontal cortex, is the source of the reward and motivation limb.

• The substantia nigra pars compacta, the pigmented lateral midbrain nucleus, is the source of the motor and habit limb that feeds the dorsal striatum.

• The ventral striatum including the nucleus accumbens, the input hub of the reward loop, is where prediction errors first bias approach and incentive.

• The dorsal striatum, caudate and putamen, is where the same errors slowly carve stimulus-response habits and set movement vigour.

• The prefrontal cortex, the frontal association sheet, receives a sparser dopamine supply that gates working memory and value-guided choice.


THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The mesolimbic pathway runs from the ventral tegmental area to the nucleus accumbens and amygdala; origin ventral tegmental area, destination ventral striatum, cargo prediction error and incentive salience, format phasic dopamine transients riding on a tonic baseline.

The mesocortical pathway runs from the ventral tegmental area to the prefrontal cortex; cargo value and effort signals, format a slower and sparser dopamine release read mostly by high-affinity receptors.

The nigrostriatal pathway runs from the substantia nigra pars compacta to the dorsal striatum; cargo the teaching signal for habits and the vigour set-point, format dense phasic and tonic dopamine.

At each destination the interface is not a wire but a receptor field: dopamine diffuses from release sites and is read by two families of receptor, defining the territories described next.


THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

At the source the representation is a scalar surprise: a burst above baseline when reward beats prediction, a pause below baseline when reward falls short, and no change when the world matches expectation.

The classic finding is that the burst migrates in time from the reward itself to the earliest cue that predicts it, which is exactly what a teaching signal for prediction should do.

In the striatum that scalar is split across two neuron populations by receptor affinity: the direct-pathway cells carrying the D1 receptor, which has low affinity and so responds mainly to the high phasic peaks that say better than expected, and the indirect-pathway cells carrying the D2 receptor, which has high affinity and so is driven by the tonic background and is unmasked by the dips that say worse than expected.

The hand-off therefore converts one error number into two opposite lessons: strengthen the action that beat expectation, weaken the action that disappointed.

By the time it reaches habit, the error has been integrated over thousands of trials into a stored policy, a fixed map from situation to action that no longer needs the teacher present.


THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

At source the system runs a pacemaker motif, an intrinsically bursting cell that free-runs at a low rate and is kicked into bursts by excitatory input.

At the striatum it runs the three-factor plasticity motif, in which a synapse changes only when presynaptic activity, postsynaptic activity, and the dopamine signal coincide, the biological form of reinforcement learning.

The direct-versus-indirect opponent motif, two populations pushing a shared output in opposite directions, does the selection work.


TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Tonic firing is a slow pacemaker rhythm of roughly 0.5 to 8 hertz that sets the ambient dopamine level over seconds to minutes.

Phasic bursts exceed 15 to 20 hertz and last 100 to 500 milliseconds, producing the teaching transient.

The reward prediction error transient itself is fast, peaking within about 100 milliseconds of a surprising cue, while the vigour and motivation effects of tonic dopamine build and decay over tens of seconds to minutes.


CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

The human midbrain holds on the order of 400,000 to 600,000 dopamine neurons total across both sides, a tiny population steering a striatum of many hundreds of millions of neurons.

The prediction error is close to a single scalar per moment, a very low bandwidth for so consequential a signal.

Because one axon can carry hundreds of thousands of release sites, the arborisation is enormous: a single human dopamine neuron is estimated to support on the order of one to four million synaptic contacts, which is also why these cells are metabolically fragile.


MODULATION AND GATING:

The system is gated at its source by inputs from the pedunculopontine and laterodorsal tegmental nuclei, which supply the acetylcholine and glutamate that convert pacemaker firing into bursts.

It is retuned by the striatal cholinergic interneurons, whose pause after salient cues shapes how dopamine is read, and by inhibitory feedback from the ventral pallidum and lateral habenula, the latter driving the dips that signal worse than expected.

Stress hormones and the orexin system raise its excitability, biasing the whole economy towards action.


PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Dopamine is itself the plasticity signal for much of the forebrain: it sets the sign and size of long-term potentiation and depression at corticostriatal synapses, so that the same paired activity strengthens a synapse when dopamine is high and weakens it when dopamine is low.

This is the machinery of habit formation, incentive learning, and the slow automation of skill.

The system also learns about itself, adjusting the size of its own bursts as predictions improve, so that a fully predicted reward eventually evokes no teaching signal at all.


DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Midbrain dopamine neurons are born early in gestation from the ventral midbrain floor plate under the control of the transcription factors known by the short names FOXA2, LMX1A, and NURR1.

Their axons reach the striatum before birth and continue to elaborate the vast arbor through childhood.

The prefrontal dopamine supply matures late, continuing to remodel through adolescence, which is one reason value-guided self-control is a late-arriving faculty.


EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

A dopamine-based reward and approach system is ancient and conserved: even the worm and the fly use dopamine to signal reward and to modulate movement.

The vertebrate innovation is the split into a nigrostriatal habit limb and a mesocorticolimbic value limb, which scales with the expansion of the striatum and frontal cortex.

The teaching-signal logic is old enough that engineered reinforcement learning rediscovered it independently.


THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

The system is sensitive to the metabolic hormones: insulin and leptin, which report energy stores, dampen dopamine reward signalling when the body is fed, while the gut hormone ghrelin, which reports hunger, amplifies it.

Cortisol, the adrenal stress hormone, sensitises dopamine release and shifts the balance towards habit over goal-directed control.

Gonadal hormones, especially oestradiol, modulate striatal dopamine release across the reproductive cycle.


STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Awake and engaged, the system runs its full phasic teaching mode.

In slow-wave sleep, phasic reward signalling is largely quiet, though tonic activity persists and dopamine appears to help stamp in the day's rewarded learning during offline replay.

In rapid-eye-movement sleep, ventral tegmental dopamine activity rises and is thought to contribute to the emotional and rewarding texture of dreams.

Under stress, tonic dopamine climbs and choice shifts from deliberate to habitual.


DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Death of the substantia nigra pars compacta neurons causes Parkinson disease, whose slowness, rigidity, and tremor are the loss of the vigour set-point, appearing only after roughly 50 to 70 percent of these cells are gone.

Excess or dysregulated dopamine signalling is implicated in the positive symptoms of schizophrenia, which antipsychotic drugs treat by blocking the D2 receptor.

Hijacking of the teaching signal by drugs of abuse produces addiction, in which the prediction error is captured by the drug cue.


HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

Remove this system and the organism is not merely sad but inert: it still perceives reward and can still move, yet it will not initiate effort to obtain reward, the state seen in advanced Parkinson disease and in profound apathy.

Turn it up and the world glows with possibility and every cue seems to promise a payoff, the state of mania and of stimulant intoxication.

The dopamine level is, in a real sense, the organism's estimate of how good it is worth being alive right now.


SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Reward and salience signals from the lateral hypothalamus, pedunculopontine nucleus, and cortex; a worse-than-expected teaching input from the lateral habenula; metabolic state from insulin, leptin, and ghrelin.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).

◦ A reward prediction error and a tonic vigour level to the striatum and prefrontal cortex, read by D1 and D2 receptor families.

• STATE.

◦ Tonic firing rate, phasic burst probability, ambient extracellular dopamine concentration, receptor occupancy on direct and indirect pathways.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Learning rate set by burst size; discount and vigour set by tonic level; D1 low-affinity and D2 high-affinity thresholds; roughly 400,000 to 600,000 source neurons.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Phasic burst 100 to 500 milliseconds; prediction error peak near 100 milliseconds; tonic motivational drift tens of seconds to minutes.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Nigral cell loss produces Parkinson disease; D2 overtransmission produces the psychosis of schizophrenia; teaching-signal capture produces addiction.


THE ENGINEERING NOTE:

As a engineering pipeline the system is named reward_teaching_system.

It subscribes to reward_outcome and reward_prediction topics and publishes a prediction_error topic plus a tonic vigour_level parameter that other pipelines read.

Its nearest engineering analogue is the temporal-difference learner at the heart of reinforcement learning, which computes exactly this error and uses it to update value estimates.

The biological difference is that the update is not a clean write to a memory address but a diffuse chemical signal that reweights millions of synapses probabilistically and asymmetrically across two opposed populations.


ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to compute the temporal-difference reward prediction error that the dopamine burst broadcasts, and to use it to update a stored value.

Create a number variable called value estimate with initial value 0.

Create a number variable called learning rate with initial value 0.2.

Create a number variable called discount with initial value 0.95.

For each step in the episode, perform the following actions.

    Observe the reward received at this step.

    Observe the value estimate of the next state.

    Calculate the sum of the reward and the product of discount and the next value.

    Calculate the difference of that sum and the current value estimate, and call it the prediction error.

    Set the dopamine burst to be large and positive when the prediction error is positive, and a dip when it is negative.

    Calculate the product of learning rate and the prediction error.

    Set value estimate to the sum of value estimate and that product.

End the loop.

The procedure is complete.


CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the reward prediction error is one short line and the migration of the signal from reward to cue is best shown by simply printing the error at each step.

# Temporal-difference reward-prediction-error, the dopamine teaching signal.
# The error moves from the reward to the earliest predictive cue as value
# is learned (Schultz, Dayan & Montague, Science 1997).
LEARNING_RATE = 0.2
DISCOUNT = 0.95

def td_learn(rewards, trials=20):
    values = [0.0] * (len(rewards) + 1)   # value of each state in the trial
    for _ in range(trials):
        for t in range(len(rewards)):
            target = rewards[t] + DISCOUNT * values[t + 1]
            error = target - values[t]              # the dopamine signal
            values[t] += LEARNING_RATE * error
    return values

# A cue at step 1 reliably predicts a reward of 1.0 delivered at step 3.
rewards = [0.0, 0.0, 1.0, 0.0]
values = td_learn(rewards)
print("learned state values:", [round(v, 2) for v in values])
# After learning, the positive error has shifted to the predictive cue.
first = [rewards[t] + DISCOUNT * values[t + 1] - values[t] for t in range(4)]
print("residual errors once learned:", [round(e, 2) for e in first])


WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A few hundred thousand pigmented midbrain cells whose bursts encode the surprise in reward and migrate from the reward to its predictor, split by receptor affinity into a strengthen-and-weaken pair, and whose slow background level sets how hard the whole organism is willing to try.


CHAPTER 32. THE NORADRENALINE SYSTEM (The Locus Coeruleus Noradrenergic Projection) (The Gain-and-Reset Broadcast) (COG)

A cluster of blue-tinged cells no bigger than a grain of rice, one on each side of the upper brainstem, sends a single axon that branches until it touches nearly every part of the forebrain, and with one chemical it decides how sharply the whole brain is listening.

This is the arousal dial and the panic button in one nucleus.

Gain names its everyday work: it steepens the response of target neurons so that strong signals get stronger and weak ones fade, sharpening the contrast of whatever the brain is doing.

Reset names its emergency work: a sudden burst that interrupts ongoing processing and lets target networks reorganise around a new priority.

Broadcast names its reach: from about fifty thousand cells to the entire cortex, the definition of a volume-transmission system.


WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT ACCOMPLISHES:

The noradrenaline system is the set of brainstem neurons that make noradrenaline, a catecholamine signalling molecule also called norepinephrine, and release it almost everywhere in the brain to set arousal and to tune the responsiveness of target circuits.

Noradrenaline is spelled out here as the British name for the transmitter that American sources call norepinephrine; the two words name the same molecule.

Its central accomplishment is gain modulation, changing how strongly neurons respond to their inputs without changing the inputs themselves, so that the same cortex can be sluggish, optimally responsive, or frantically overexcited depending on one broadcast.

Its second accomplishment is the network reset, a phasic interruption that clears the current processing state and lets the brain reconfigure when something important changes.


THE CHAIN OF STATIONS (THE LAYER 9 ROSTER):

• The locus coeruleus, the small pigmented pontine nucleus, is the sole source of noradrenaline for almost the entire forebrain and the origin of the broadcast.

• The prefrontal cortex, the frontal association sheet, is a privileged target whose working memory and control functions follow an inverted-U dependence on noradrenaline level.

• The thalamus, the sensory relay hub, is retuned to switch between relay and rhythmic burst modes.

• The amygdala, the threat-salience nucleus, both drives and receives the broadcast, coupling arousal to emotion.

• The whole neocortex is the ultimate territory, receiving diffuse noradrenaline that sets its signal-to-noise ratio.


THE PATHWAYS AND INTERFACES:

The dorsal noradrenergic bundle runs from the locus coeruleus forward through the midbrain to the thalamus, hippocampus, and entire neocortex; origin locus coeruleus, destination forebrain, cargo an arousal and gain signal, format tonic firing with superimposed phasic bursts.

Descending branches from the same cells reach the spinal cord and cerebellum, so one neuron can influence both cognition and posture.

The interface at each target is the adrenoceptor field: high-affinity alpha-2 receptors that respond to low tonic levels, moderate-affinity alpha-1 receptors, and lower-affinity beta receptors recruited by high phasic levels, which is why the same transmitter helps at moderate levels and harms at high ones.


THE TRANSFORMATION ALONG THE CHAIN:

At the source the representation is a two-mode arousal signal: a tonic firing rate that reports the general level of alertness, and phasic bursts locked to behaviourally significant, decision-relevant events.

The adaptive gain theory reads these two modes as a trade-off: moderate tonic firing with crisp phasic bursts supports focused, exploitative attention on a task, while high tonic firing without phasic bursts supports distractible, exploratory scanning.

In the target cortex the signal is transformed into a change of neuronal gain, mathematically like steepening the slope of each neuron's input-output curve, which sharpens the difference between attended and ignored signals.

The network reset reading adds that a large phasic burst does not just sharpen but interrupts, releasing target networks from their current attractor so they can settle into a new one appropriate to the changed world.


THE MOTIFS IT RUNS (THE LAYER 8 INVENTORY):

At source it runs a coupled-oscillator motif, a set of electrically connected pacemaker cells that can fire in near-synchrony for a global broadcast.

At target it runs the gain-modulation motif, multiplying rather than adding to a neuron's drive.

The reset is the attractor-destabilisation motif, a transient input that ejects a network from one stable state so it can find another.


TEMPORAL SIGNATURE:

Tonic firing runs at roughly 1 to 3 hertz and drifts with the arousal state over seconds to minutes.

Phasic bursts of 8 to 20 hertz last a few hundred milliseconds and follow salient events by about 100 to 200 milliseconds.

The gain change they produce outlasts the burst by seconds, and the arousal drift that governs the exploration-exploitation balance plays out over minutes.


CAPACITY AND LIMITS:

The human locus coeruleus holds on the order of 45,000 to 50,000 noradrenaline neurons per side, tens of thousands of cells for the whole cortex.

Its output is nearly scalar, a single global arousal number, which is why it cannot carry content, only set the mode.

Its dependence on target function is an inverted U: too little noradrenaline and the cortex is drowsy and inattentive, too much and it is anxious and disorganised, with a narrow optimum in between.


MODULATION AND GATING:

The nucleus is driven by the orexin system, which supplies a wakefulness command, and by the amygdala and the nucleus paragigantocellularis, which report threat.

It is restrained by its own alpha-2 autoreceptors, the target of clonidine and of the attention medicine guanfacine.

Corticotropin-releasing hormone, the opening signal of the stress axis, excites it, wiring arousal directly to stress.


PLASTICITY AND LEARNING:

Noradrenaline gates plasticity, especially through beta-adrenoceptors that lower the threshold for long-term potentiation, which is why emotionally arousing events are remembered better and why a phasic burst can tag a moment as worth storing.

In the orbitofrontal cortex, noradrenaline supports the adjustment of the learning rate itself, letting the brain learn faster when the environment has just changed and more slowly when it is stable.

This makes the system a meta-learner, setting how fast other systems learn.


DEVELOPMENT AND ASSEMBLY:

Locus coeruleus neurons arise early from the dorsal rhombencephalon under the transcription factors known by the short names PHOX2A and PHOX2B.

Their axons pioneer some of the earliest long-range tracts in the developing brain and help pattern cortical maturation.

The nucleus is among the first to show the tau pathology of Alzheimer disease decades before symptoms, making its slow attrition a lifelong feature.


EVOLUTIONARY PROVENANCE:

A single central arousal nucleus using a catecholamine is conserved across vertebrates, and analogous octopamine systems tune arousal in insects.

The mammalian locus coeruleus is remarkable for how few cells govern how much territory, a design that trades content for reach.

Its coupling to stress and to memory is ancient, tying fast bodily arousal to the marking of important events.


THE ENDOCRINE COUPLING:

The system is the central partner of the peripheral fight-or-flight response, running in parallel with adrenal adrenaline released into the blood.

Corticotropin-releasing hormone and cortisol from the stress axis both excite and sensitise it, so chronic stress leaves it chronically overactive.

It in turn drives the autonomic outflow that sets heart rate and blood pressure, closing a loop between brain arousal and body arousal.


STATES AND THE LAYER 11 SCHEDULE:

Awake and alert, the locus coeruleus fires tonically with task-locked phasic bursts.

In slow-wave sleep its firing falls low, permitting the cortex to enter slow oscillations.

In rapid-eye-movement sleep it falls essentially silent, a near-complete shutdown thought to be necessary for that state and for the recalibration of adrenoceptors.

Under acute stress it fires hard and tonically, producing the hypervigilant, distractible state of alarm.


DISEASES AND DISORDERS:

Overactivity and dysregulation of the system contribute to anxiety disorders and to post-traumatic stress disorder, where the reset and arousal functions are stuck on.

Early degeneration of the locus coeruleus is a feature of both Alzheimer disease and Parkinson disease and contributes to their attentional and mood symptoms.

Attention-deficit hyperactivity disorder is treated in part by drugs that raise noradrenaline signalling towards the optimum of the inverted U.


HOW IT COLOURS THE ORGANISM:

This system sets the emotional temperature of experience: at its optimum the world feels crisp and worth attending to, below it the world feels dull and far away, above it the world feels threatening and demands escape.

It is the difference between reading a book calmly and reading the same page while a fire alarm sounds.

The noradrenaline level is the organism's estimate of how urgently the present moment must be attended to.


SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• SUBSCRIBES (INPUTS).

◦ Wakefulness drive from orexin; threat and salience from the amygdala and nucleus paragigantocellularis; stress from corticotropin-releasing hormone; unexpected-change signals from cortex.

• PUBLISHES (OUTPUTS).
